Hierarchy Designer/Overview

MSUltimate - Hierarchy Designer Documentation

Transform the Unity Hierarchy into an Informative, Beautiful, and Diagnostic Game Architecture Tree.
Standalone Addon for Unity 6 (6000.x)+.

Overview

MSUltimate Hierarchy Designer revolutionizes Unity's native Hierarchy window. It provides visual folders, tree branch connectors, component icon badges, active/visibility toggles, live memory/draw call telemetry, lock icons, and rich typography.

Browse by Category

Foundational hierarchy window decorators: active buttons, asset thumbnails, badges, component counters, conditional highlights, enum pickers, field alerts, flashes, highlights, icons, steppers, labels, and layer selectors.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyActiveButton]

[HierarchyActiveButton]

Draws an interactive clickable power toggle button directly on the hierarchy row to enable or disable GameObjects with one click.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyActiveButton] embeds a fast active/inactive toggle switch directly onto the right edge of a GameObject's hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyActiveButtonRenderer, it displays an interactive visibility or power icon (Size = 14f) that toggles go.SetActive(!isActive) upon clicking. The icon dynamically updates between active (ActiveIcon = "d_scenevis_visible_hover") and inactive states (InactiveIcon = "d_scenevis_hidden_hover"), complete with custom color tinting (InactiveColor = "#FF6B6B"). It records full Unity Undo operations in Edit Mode and executes instantly during Play Mode testing.

The Problem: Toggling GameObjects on and off is one of the most frequent actions in Unity level design and debugging. Without an inline button, developers must select the GameObject in the Hierarchy, shift their gaze and mouse over to the Inspector, click the tiny checkbox in the top-left corner, and then return to the Hierarchy.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyActiveButton]:

  • Single-Click Activation: Turn entities on or off instantly from the Hierarchy without selecting them or moving your cursor to the Inspector.
  • Clear Visual State Feedback: Inactive objects stand out clearly with distinct red-tinted hidden icons (#FF6B6B).
  • Complete Undo Integration: In Edit Mode, all toggles register with Unity's Undo stack (Undo.RecordObject) and mark scenes dirty for seamless saving.
  • Play Mode Responsive: Works fluidly during Play Mode gameplay sessions to toggle spawners, lights, and UI canvases in real time.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyActiveButton]
public class AmbientLightingGroup : MonoBehaviour
{
    public Light[] streetLamps;
    public float ambientIntensity = 1.2f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyActiveButton(
    ActiveIcon = "d_winbtn_mac_max", 
    InactiveIcon = "d_winbtn_mac_close", 
    InactiveColor = "#EF4444", 
    Size = 14f, 
    XOffset = 4f)]
public class WaveSpawnerController : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Spawner Configuration")]
    public GameObject enemyPrefab;
    public int enemiesPerWave = 12;
    public float waveIntervalSeconds = 25f;

    [Header("Telemetry")]
    public int currentWaveNumber = 1;
    public bool isSpawningActive = true;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyAssetThumbnail]

[HierarchyAssetThumbnail]

Renders an interactive asset preview thumbnail directly in the hierarchy row with hover zoom popouts and single-click asset pinging.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyAssetThumbnail] brings asset previews out of the Project and Inspector windows and directly into the Scene Hierarchy. Applied to an asset reference field (such as Sprite, Texture2D, Mesh, Material, or AudioClip), it renders an interactive preview badge (ThumbnailSize = 16f, CornerRadius = 3f) on the object's hierarchy row. If the asset is missing, it draws a warning border (MissingBorderColor = "#552222"). Hovering the cursor displays a magnified preview popout (ShowHoverPopout = true, PopoutSize = 64f), and clicking the thumbnail immediately pings and highlights the asset in the Project window (AllowPingOnClick = true).

The Problem: Verifying whether UI icons, character sprites, audio stingers, or material variants are assigned across dozens of hierarchy entities requires selecting each GameObject and scouring the Inspector. Missing asset references frequently go unnoticed until runtime rendering failures occur.

The Solution: Decorating serialized asset fields with [HierarchyAssetThumbnail]:

  • At-a-Glance Asset Auditing: Scan through the entire scene hierarchy to confirm that sprites, models, and materials are assigned correctly.
  • Single-Click Project Ping: Click the thumbnail badge to immediately highlight and locate the referenced source asset in your Project directory.
  • Magnified Hover Popout: Hover over the miniature icon to view high-resolution 64px asset previews without opening secondary windows.
  • Instant Missing-Reference Alerts: Null fields display distinct dark-red warning borders, catching unassigned references instantly.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class InventoryItemSlotUI : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyAssetThumbnail(ThumbnailSize = 16f, ShowHoverPopout = true)]
    public Sprite itemIcon;

    public string itemName = "Health Potion";
    public int stackCount = 5;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class EnvironmentPropAnchor : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Visual Meshes")]
    [Tooltip("Primary 3D mesh model rendered on this prop.")]
    [HierarchyAssetThumbnail(ThumbnailSize = 18f, ShowHoverPopout = true, PopoutSize = 80f, BorderColor = "#3B82F6")]
    public Mesh propModel;

    [Tooltip("Primary surface material.")]
    [HierarchyAssetThumbnail(ThumbnailSize = 18f, XOffset = 24f)]
    public Material propMaterial;

    [Header("Prop Metadata")]
    public bool isDestructible = true;
    public float healthPoints = 100f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyBadge]

[HierarchyBadge]

Draws a sleek colored pill badge on the hierarchy row for static entity categorization or dynamic conditional state display.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBadge] decorates hierarchy items with sharp, readable metadata pills. Rendered via HierarchyBadgeRenderer, it can be placed on a class for static categorization (Text = "BOSS", BadgeColor = "#DC2626") or on a serialized field for live conditional state monitoring (ShowCondition = HierarchyBadgeCondition.WhenTrue). When bound to fields, it supports dynamic toggling between primary and alternate text and colors (AlternateText = "DEAD", AlternateBadgeColor = "#374151"), giving instant visual telemetry without selecting the object.

The Problem: Hierarchy rows only show the GameObject name. As scene trees grow to thousands of entities, determining which entity is an AI NavMesh agent, a Network Player, an Environmental Hazard, or a Spawn Point requires reading naming conventions or opening component lists.

The Solution: Decorating classes and serialized state variables with [HierarchyBadge]:

  • Instant Visual Identification: Categorize entities with clean status pills (e.g. "AUDIO", "UI", "PLAYER", "LIGHT").
  • Dynamic Field-State Monitoring: Automatically flips badges when bool fields change (e.g. "READY" vs "CHARGING").
  • Customizable Pill Typography: Control font sizes, bold weights, rounded corners (BorderRadius = 4f), and custom text hues.
  • Zero GC Footprint: Fast layout calculations with zero runtime memory allocations during hierarchy repaints.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyBadge(Text = "PLAYER", BadgeColor = "#0284C7", TextColor = "#FFFFFF", Bold = true)]
public class PlayerCharacterEntity : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyBadge(Text = "ALIVE", AlternateText = "DEAD", BadgeColor = "#16A34A", AlternateBadgeColor = "#991B1B", ShowCondition = HierarchyBadgeCondition.WhenTrue)]
    public bool isAlive = true;

    public float currentHealth = 100f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyBadge(Text = "NETWORK SPAWN", BadgeColor = "#7C3AED", TextColor = "#F5F3FF", Bold = true, XOffset = 4f)]
public class NetworkSpawnAnchor : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Network Authority")]
    public int networkPlayerId = 1;

    [Header("Live Connection State")]
    [HierarchyBadge(
        Text = "CONNECTED", 
        AlternateText = "DISCONNECTED", 
        BadgeColor = "#059669", 
        AlternateBadgeColor = "#DC2626", 
        ShowCondition = HierarchyBadgeCondition.WhenTrue, 
        XOffset = 95f)]
    public bool isClientConnected = true;

    public float roundTripPingMs = 38.5f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyComponentCount]

[HierarchyComponentCount]

Displays the total attached component count directly on the hierarchy row with customizable thresholds to catch bloated GameObjects.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyComponentCount] audits GameObject complexity directly within the scene hierarchy. Rendered via HierarchyComponentCountRenderer, it counts all attached components (excluding Transform by default via ExcludeTransform = true) and displays a compact numerical counter badge (e.g. "6C"). When component density exceeds a healthy architectural threshold (HighCountThreshold = 8), the badge dynamically shifts to a prominent warning alert (HighCountColor = "#552222", HighCountTextColor = "#FF8888"), highlighting bloated entities that require refactoring.

The Problem: In large projects, GameObjects frequently become monolithic "God Objects" accumulating 10 to 20 unrelated components (scripts, colliders, audio sources, particle emitters). Because component counts are only visible by selecting the object and scrolling through the Inspector, architectural bloat goes unnoticed until performance degrades.

The Solution: Tagging managers and core entities with [HierarchyComponentCount]:

  • At-a-Glance Architectural Auditing: Scan your hierarchy tree to instantly spot GameObjects carrying excessive components.
  • Configurable Warning Thresholds: Set component limits (e.g., alert at 8+ components) to enforce clean component composition across teams.
  • Smart Filtering: Ignores the ubiquitous Transform component (ExcludeTransform = true) and suppresses badges on simple objects (MinCountToShow = 2).
  • Zero Runtime Performance Impact: Lightweight editor-only reflection that refreshes cleanly upon component addition or removal.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyComponentCount(MinCountToShow = 2, HighCountThreshold = 6)]
public class ModularActorController : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float movementSpeed = 6.5f;
    public bool isGrounded = true;
}

3. Compatibility & Specification

SpecificationValue

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyComponentCount(
    ExcludeTransform = true, 
    MinCountToShow = 3, 
    HighCountThreshold = 7, 
    HighCountColor = "#7F1D1D", 
    HighCountTextColor = "#FCA5A5", 
    Suffix = " Comps", 
    XOffset = 6f)]
public class CombatVehicleMasterEntity : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Vehicle Subsystems")]
    public float engineHorsepower = 850f;
    public float armorThicknessMm = 120f;
    public bool isTurretOperational = true;
}

7. Stacking & Compatibility Matrix

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyConditionalHighlight]

[HierarchyConditionalHighlight]

Dynamically tints the hierarchy row background with customizable colors based on live boolean states or numeric thresholds.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyConditionalHighlight] turns the hierarchy list into a responsive telemetry dashboard. Applied to a serialized field, HierarchyConditionalHighlightRenderer monitors field values in real time and paints a dynamic background highlight across the entire row (FullRow = true) or a right-side indicator band (BandWidth = 80f). For booleans, it displays distinct colors for true and false states (TrueColor = "#1A3A1A"). For numeric fields, it compares against a threshold (Threshold = 50f) or smoothly interpolates color gradients (GradientMode = true, MaxValue = 100f).

The Problem: During playtesting and simulation debugging, developers must monitor critical runtime states (e.g., whether an AI has spotted the player, whether a boss shield is active, or whether a vehicle is out of fuel). Watching numeric fields in the Inspector is impossible when debugging multiple entities simultaneously.

The Solution: Decorating critical runtime variables with [HierarchyConditionalHighlight]:

  • Live Scene Telemetry: Spot state changes instantly across dozens of entities in the hierarchy without selecting them.
  • Dual Operating Modes: Native support for boolean flags (TrueColor / FalseColor) and numerical thresholds (Threshold / BelowColor).
  • Smooth Gradient Mapping: Map health or temperature from 0% to 100% across color gradients for dynamic heatmaps.
  • Full Row or Right Band: Choose between full-row background tinting or unobtrusive right-hand edge color bands.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyEnumCycler]

[HierarchyEnumCycler]

Displays an enum field as an interactive badge on the hierarchy row that cycles through enum values on click with full Undo support.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyEnumCycler] equips hierarchy rows with a rapid one-click enum state cycler. Applied to an enum field, HierarchyEnumCyclerRenderer renders the current enum value inside an interactive badge pill (BadgeColor = "#1E3A5F", TextColor = "#64B5F6"). Left-clicking the badge advances the field to the next enum entry, while right-clicking cycles in reverse (RightClickReverse = true, WrapAround = true). Changes register full Undo operations in Edit Mode and update instant state in Play Mode.

The Problem: Switching states—such as AI combat behaviors (Idle, Patrol, Alert, Combat), team affiliations (Red, Blue, Neutral), or lighting modes—requires selecting each entity, locating the enum dropdown in the Inspector, and clicking through a menu. Toggling states across several dozen entities is slow and tedious.

The Solution: Decorating enum state fields with [HierarchyEnumCycler]:

  • Single-Click Value Cycling: Step through enum modes instantly with a single mouse click directly in the hierarchy.
  • Bi-Directional Navigation: Left-click steps forward; right-click steps backward through enum values.
  • Continuous Wrapping: Loops automatically from the last enum value back to the first (WrapAround = true).
  • Complete Undo Integration: Every click creates a registered Undo step in Edit Mode, allowing effortless batch rollbacks.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public enum AIState { Idle, Patrol, Investigate, Combat }

public class GuardDroneAI : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyEnumCycler(BadgeColor = "#1E3A5F", TextColor = "#93C5FD")]
    public AIState currentBehavior = AIState.Patrol;

    public float detectionRadiusMeters = 15f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyEnumSelector]

[HierarchyEnumSelector]

Injects an inline dropdown popup menu directly on the hierarchy row to pick any enum value with direct random access.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyEnumSelector] brings full dropdown popup navigation directly into the Scene Hierarchy. Rendered by HierarchyEnumSelectorRenderer, it draws an elegant dropdown box widget (Width = 80f, BadgeColor = "#1E2B3A", TextColor = "#7AAFCC") on the hierarchy row displaying the current enum value. Clicking the widget opens Unity's native context popup menu listing all declared enum members, allowing developers to jump directly to any desired state with full Undo support.

The Problem: While [HierarchyEnumCycler] is ideal for enums with 2 to 4 options, enums with 6, 10, or 20 values (such as Weapon Type, Sound Channel, Animation Phase, or Monster Archetype) become frustrating to cycle through sequentially one click at a time.

The Solution: Decorating extensive enum fields with [HierarchyEnumSelector]:

  • Direct Random Access: Open a full dropdown menu with one click and pick any enum state immediately.
  • Sleek Embedded Widget: Formatted as a subtle inline selector with rounded corners and clear typography.
  • Native Context Menu Integration: Uses standard Unity GenericMenu popups familiar to all developers.
  • Complete Undo Integrity: Value changes register instant Undo records in Edit Mode and update live variables during Play Mode.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyFieldAlert]

[HierarchyFieldAlert]

Draws an attention-grabbing warning icon and pulsing background overlay on the hierarchy row when a field value violates defined constraints.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyFieldAlert] turns the hierarchy list into an active error and outlier surveillance radar. Powered by HierarchyFieldAlertRenderer, it monitors numeric bounds (MinValue = 0f, MaxValue = float.MaxValue) or boolean criteria (AlertWhenFalse = true). When the field violates these rules, it renders a pulsing alert icon (AlertIcon = "d_console.erroricon", AlertIconColor = "#FF6666", Pulse = true) alongside an urgent background row tint (AlertColor = "#400000", AlertAlpha = 0.55f).

The Problem: Out-of-bounds gameplay variables—such as negative enemy health, zero weapon damage, depleted fuel tanks, or unassigned critical booleans—silently corrupt game balance. Tracking down which GameObject in a scene of 500 entities has invalid numbers usually requires manual inspection or custom console log spam.

The Solution: Decorating critical thresholds with [HierarchyFieldAlert]:

  • Immediate Hierarchy Error Surfacing: Spot corrupt, zeroed, or out-of-bounds variables in seconds without opening console logs.
  • Pulsing Animation Support: Smooth alpha oscillation (Pulse = true) ensures alerts immediately catch the developer's eye.
  • Multi-Type Validation: Seamlessly handles numeric floats/ints (minimum and maximum bounds) and boolean failure states (AlertWhenFalse).
  • Flexible Alert Scope: Choose between full row highlighting (FullRowAlert = true) or a discreet right-hand icon badge.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class EnemyHealthEntity : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyFieldAlert(MinValue = 1f, AlertColor = "#7F1D1D", AlertIconColor = "#EF4444")]
    public float currentHealth = 100f;

    public float maxHealth = 100f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyFlash]

[HierarchyFlash]

Briefly flashes the hierarchy row with an easing light strobe when a monitored boolean field transitions from false to true.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyFlash] provides transient visual feedback for discrete scene events. Handled by HierarchyFlashRenderer, it monitors a boolean trigger flag. When the flag flips from false to true, the entire hierarchy row (FullRowFlash = true) or a right-side indicator band (BandWidth = 80f) flashes with sudden peak luminescence (FlashColor = "#FF5555", AttackTime = 0.05f) before smoothly decaying away over time (Duration = 0.8f, FadeToColor = "#FF555500"). Built-in cooldowns (CooldownTime = 0.3f) and reset checks (RequireReset = true) prevent strobe spam during continuous triggers.

The Problem: Event-driven behaviors—such as trigger volumes firing, hitscan shots connecting, dialogue cues triggering, or achievement unlock pulses—occur in single frames. Because boolean state flips happen and reset in milliseconds, developers watching the Inspector or Scene view completely miss whether an event actually fired.

The Solution: Decorating event trigger booleans with [HierarchyFlash]:

  • Instant Optical Strobe Feedback: Flashes the hierarchy row the exact frame an event fires, holding attention with a gentle fadeout curve.
  • Microsecond Event Capture: Never miss single-frame pulses that reset before human eyes can catch them in standard Inspector checkboxes.
  • Configurable Flash Dynamics: Adjust peak flash hues (crimson for hits, gold for pickups, electric cyan for portals) and duration decay.
  • Anti-Spam Cooldown Guards: Built-in attack/decay easing and cooldown timers maintain smooth editor rendering even during rapid fire.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class ProximityMineTrigger : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyFlash(FlashColor = "#EF4444", Duration = 1.0f)]
    public bool isDetonated = false;

    public float explosionRadiusMeters = 8.5f;
    public int damageAmount = 250;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyHighlight]

[HierarchyHighlight]

Paints an elegant colored background tint across the hierarchy row with solid, gradient, or striped patterns.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyHighlight] structures complex scene trees into visually distinct, color-coded zones. Managed by HierarchyHighlightRenderer, it tints the full width of the GameObject's hierarchy row using customizable hex colors (ColorHex = "#4D4D4D40"). It features three aesthetic fill patterns (Mode = HierarchyHighlightMode.Solid, Gradient, or Striped), optional child inheritance (ApplyToChildren = true) that applies dimmed tints to nested hierarchy branches, and selection-gated rendering (ShowOnlyWhenSelected = true).

The Problem: In dense 3D scenes with hundreds of nested GameObjects, environmental set pieces, audio emitters, lighting rigs, and UI canvases blend into an indistinguishable gray wall of text. Finding the root object of a subsystem or distinguishing gameplay entities from static geometry requires endless scrolling.

The Solution: Color-coding scene architecture with [HierarchyHighlight]:

  • Instant Subsystem Recognition: Group lighting, audio, gameplay, and UI hierarchies with distinct color palettes.
  • Multiple Visual Fill Styles: Choose between clean solid tints, modern left-to-right gradients (GradientEndColor), or subtle diagonal hazard stripes.
  • Recursive Branch Tinting: Automatically tint child objects (ApplyToChildren = true) to visually encapsulate entire folder structures.
  • Selection Gating: Optionally reveal highlights only when an entity or parent is actively selected.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyIcon]

[HierarchyIcon]

Displays built-in or custom icon badges on the hierarchy row statically or dynamically based on field states.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyIcon] equips hierarchy rows with clear visual symbols representing component roles or runtime states. Processed by HierarchyIconRenderer, it draws any built-in Unity editor icon or custom texture (IconName = "d_FilterByType", Size = 14f) alongside the object entry. It supports custom hex tinting (Tint = "#10B981"), hover tooltips (Tooltip), multi-icon slot stacking (StackSlot = 0), and conditional field visibility rules (ShowCondition = HierarchyIconCondition.Always, WhenTrue, WhenFalse, WhenNotNull, WhenNull).

The Problem: By default, Unity shows only a generic blue cube prefab icon or an empty GameObject icon in the Hierarchy. Distinguishing cameras, light sources, trigger colliders, audio zones, or AI pathfinding nodes at a glance requires reading long name strings.

The Solution: Tagging components and field states with [HierarchyIcon]:

  • Instant Visual Semantics: Display unmistakable icons for lights, cameras, audio clips, weapons, and physics bodies.
  • Dynamic Field-State Display: Automatically reveal warning icons when a critical object reference is null (WhenNull) or when a state flag is active (WhenTrue).
  • Multi-Icon Horizontal Stacking: Stack multiple diagnostic icons side by side using deterministic StackSlot indices.
  • Custom Icon Tinting: Colorize standard monochrome editor icons to match team color coding conventions.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyIntStepper]

[HierarchyIntStepper]

Injects interactive increment and decrement stepper buttons on the hierarchy row to tune numeric variables with single clicks.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyIntStepper] brings micro-parameter adjustment controls directly into the scene tree. Rendered by HierarchyIntStepperRenderer, it displays a pair of interactive minus (-) and plus (+) stepper buttons (ButtonWidth = 16f, ButtonColor = "#2A3340") flanking a central numeric value readout (ValueFormat = "{0:F0}"). Clicking either button alters the underlying numeric field by a defined increment (Step = 1f), automatically clamped between bounds (MinValue, MaxValue), with full Undo history in Edit Mode.

The Problem: Tuning integer quantities across multiple entities—such as adjusting enemy spawn counts, coin values, waypoint sequence numbers, or inventory stack sizes—requires selecting each GameObject and typing into Inspector text boxes. Fine-tuning values across adjacent objects is slow and repetitive.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [HierarchyIntStepper]:

  • Single-Click Micro Tuning: Increment or decrement counts in seconds directly from the hierarchy row.
  • Embedded Live Value Readout: Displays the current numeric state between the stepper buttons (ValueWidth = 32f, ShowValue = true).
  • Defensive Value Clamping: Constrain values within safe numeric ranges (MinValue, MaxValue) to prevent out-of-bounds errors.
  • Complete Undo Integration: Every button click registers with Unity's Undo system, making experimentation completely risk-free.

2. Quick Start

    public int currentWaveNumber = 1;

    public float enemyHealthScale = 1.2f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyLabel]

[HierarchyLabel]

Displays static descriptive text or live serialized field values directly on the hierarchy row with custom typography and alignments.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyLabel] surfaces text strings and variable values directly into the hierarchy view. Processed by HierarchyLabelRenderer, it can render static descriptive text (Text = "Zone Anchor") when applied to a class, or automatically mirror the live runtime value of a field (FormatString = "{0:F1} m/s"). It features flexible horizontal positioning (Alignment = HierarchyLabelAlignment.Right or Left), rich typography controls (bold, italic, custom colors, and font sizes), character truncation limits (MaxLength = 16), and optional background pill framing (BackgroundColor).

The Problem: GameObject names are often terse or standardized for code (e.g. Enemy_04, LightRig_B), obscuring important human context such as target patrol zones, assigned audio busses, network IDs, or current speed metrics. Developers waste time clicking between objects just to read basic text details.

The Solution: Decorating classes and serialized values with [HierarchyLabel]:

  • Live Variable Projection: Watch string values, floats, coordinates, or states update in real time in the hierarchy list.
  • Dual Horizontal Anchoring: Pin text flush against the right edge of the row or immediately following the GameObject name on the left.
  • Rich Typography Styling: Format labels with bespoke colors, italic accents, bold weights, and custom font sizes.
  • Defensive Character Truncation: Keep hierarchy layouts neat and prevent horizontal overflow using the MaxLength property.

2. Quick Start

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyLayerSelector]

[HierarchyLayerSelector]

Draws an interactive layer selector widget directly on the hierarchy row to audit and reassign GameObject layers without opening the Inspector.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyLayerSelector] brings direct layer auditing and switching right into the scene hierarchy. Managed by HierarchyLayerSelectorRenderer, it displays a compact layer badge widget (Width = 70f, BadgeColor = "#1A2E1F", TextColor = "#7EC89A") displaying the GameObject's current layer name. Clicking the widget opens Unity's layer selection popup menu, allowing instant reassignment. Developers can optionally hide the badge when the object is on the Default layer (HideDefault = true) to minimize clutter, and choose whether layer changes recursively apply to children (ApplyToChildren = true).

The Problem: Layer mismatches are a leading cause of physics raycast failures, camera culling glitches, post-processing volume errors, and UI event blocking. Finding which entities are assigned to Ignore Raycast, PostProcessing, Water, or UI layers requires clicking through every object in the hierarchy and checking the top-right Inspector dropdown.

The Solution: Decorating physics, camera, and lighting components with [HierarchyLayerSelector]:

  • At-a-Glance Layer Auditing: Scan your hierarchy tree to immediately see which layer each entity belongs to.
  • Single-Click Layer Reassignment: Click the badge to open the native layer popup menu and switch layers instantly.
  • Recursive Child Support: Optionally propagate layer changes down entire child hierarchies (ApplyToChildren = true).
  • De-clutter with HideDefault: Keep your hierarchy tidy by hiding the badge on standard unspecialized objects (HideDefault = true).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyLayerSelector(Width = 75f, HideDefault = true)]
public class RaycastHitTarget : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float surfaceArmor = 50f;
    public bool isPenetrable = false;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyLayerTag]

[HierarchyLayerTag]

Displays the GameObject's assigned Unity layer as a clean label or colored badge directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyLayerTag] reads the GameObject's current layer (LayerMask.LayerToName(go.layer)) and renders it directly on the right edge of the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyLayerTagRenderer, it can display the layer either as a subtle italicized text label (ShowAsBadge = false) or as a rounded pill badge (ShowAsBadge = true). It supports auto-hiding for standard default objects (HideDefault = true), custom color themes, font overrides, and text prefixes (e.g., "L: ").

The Problem: In complex game scenes (physics colliders, raycast targets, camera culling zones, post-processing triggers, lighting layers), knowing which layer an object belongs to is critical. Without hierarchy visibility, developers must select each GameObject and check the small dropdown in the top-right corner of the Inspector, slowing down scene audits and leading to accidental layer mismatch bugs.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyLayerTag]:

  • Instant Layer Awareness: Immediately identify layer assignments (e.g. PostProcessing, Ignore Raycast, Water, Enemy) at a glance.
  • Uncluttered Defaults: Automatically hides when the object is assigned to layer 0 (Default), keeping standard hierarchy items clean.
  • Dual Display Modes: Choose between lightweight italic text or stylized rounded badges with distinct background colors.
  • Zero Reflection Overhead: Directly queries Unity's native go.layer integer without inspecting component fields or reflection caches.

2. Quick Start

csharp

public class CullingVolume : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float cullingDistance = 150f;
    public LayerMask affectedLayers;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyMinMaxTracker]

[HierarchyMinMaxTracker]

Tracks and displays the observed minimum and maximum runtime values for numeric fields directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyMinMaxTracker] attaches to numeric fields (float, int, double) and records the historical minimum and maximum values encountered during runtime execution. Driven by HierarchyMinMaxTrackerRenderer, it displays a live range indicator (Min ~ Max, e.g. 12.4 ~ 89.1) on the right side of the hierarchy row. It supports custom format strings, distinct color coding for min and max bounds, optional live value display, background boxes, and automatic state reset whenever entering Play Mode.

The Problem: When balancing gameplay mechanics (character movement speeds, frame deltas, weapon recoil, network ping, AI decision weights), numeric values fluctuate continuously. Profiling these values usually requires setting up debug charts, checking Profiler counters, or cluttering the console with spammy Debug.Log statements, which can drop frame rates.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [HierarchyMinMaxTracker]:

  • Continuous Boundary Observation: Tracks extremes seamlessly without requiring extra code or debug logging.
  • Glanceable Hierarchy Telemetry: Inspect fluctuating gameplay metrics directly in the hierarchy while keeping focus on the Scene View.
  • Independent Per-Instance Caching: Tracks each component instance separately via unique InstanceID keys.
  • Auto-Reset on Play: Automatically clears previous session bounds on entering Play Mode (ResetOnPlay = true).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class PlayerMovement : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyMinMaxTracker(ValueFormat = "{0:F1} m/s", MinColor = "#38BDF8", MaxColor = "#F43F5E")]
    public float currentSpeed;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class NetworkLatencyMonitor : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Network Telemetry")]
    [HierarchyMinMaxTracker(
        Separator = " → ", 
        ValueFormat = "{0:F0}ms", 
        ShowCurrentValue = true, 
        MinColor = "#10B981", 
        MaxColor = "#EF4444", 
        BackgroundColor = "#1E293B", 
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public float roundTripTimeMs;

    private void Update()
    {
        // Simulated network jitter
        roundTripTimeMs = 30f + Mathf.PingPong(Time.time * 25f, 60f) + Random.Range(-5f, 15f);
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyNote]

[HierarchyNote]

Attaches a documentary note and indicator icon to the hierarchy row with hover tooltip and inline text preview.

What it does: [HierarchyNote] allows developers and technical designers to anchor production reminders, architectural instructions, and warning notes directly to GameObjects in the Hierarchy window. The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyNote]:

  • Optional Inline Text Snippet: Show the first 24+ characters inline to identify key notes at a glance.
  • Custom Iconography & Tints: Choose distinct Unity icons (e.g., warning icons, info tags, bookmark symbols) and colors.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyObjectField]

[HierarchyObjectField]

Draws an interactive object reference field directly on the hierarchy row supporting click-to-ping and drag-and-drop assignment.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyObjectField] exposes any UnityEngine.Object-derived field (GameObject, Component, ScriptableObject, Material, AudioClip, etc.) as an interactive reference slot embedded directly in the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyObjectFieldRenderer, it wraps EditorGUI.ObjectField inside a sleek, custom-styled container. It renders missing references with alert backgrounds (MissingColor = "#2A1A1A"), highlights populated slots, allows drag-and-drop asset assignment, and lets developers click the field to immediately ping the referenced asset in the Project window or Scene.

The Problem: Connecting key assets (prefabs to spawners, target transforms to cameras, sound banks to audio triggers) often requires selecting an object, pinning the Inspector, and dragging assets across distant windows. When references go missing (null reference bugs), scanning dozens of game objects in the hierarchy to find which slot is empty is tedious.

The Solution: Decorating reference fields with [HierarchyObjectField]:

  • Direct Hierarchy Assignment: Drag prefabs or scene objects straight into the hierarchy row without selecting the target GameObject.
  • Instant Click-to-Ping: Click the slot name to ping and highlight the referenced object in the Project window or Scene hierarchy.
  • Visual Null Alerting: Highlights unassigned slots in deep red/alert tints so missing references can never be overlooked.
  • Full Undo Support: Records standard Unity Undo transactions in Edit Mode so changes can be reverted with Ctrl+Z.

2. Quick Start

csharp

    [HierarchyObjectField(Width = 110f)]
    public Transform followTarget;

[HierarchyStatusDot]HighPlace status dots on the row to reflect overall system readiness alongside reference slots.
Supported TypesHighWorks with any type deriving from UnityEngine.Object (GameObject, ScriptableObject, MonoBehaviour, etc.).
Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyProgressBar]

[HierarchyProgressBar]

Renders an interactive horizontal progress bar directly on the hierarchy row for numeric fields with dynamic bounds and threshold alerts.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyProgressBar] renders a sleek, miniature progress bar directly on the hierarchy row for numeric fields (float, int). Driven by HierarchyProgressBarRenderer, it dynamically visualizes fill ratios based on fixed or reflected bounds (MaxValue, MaxValueField, MinValue, MinValueField). It supports low and high threshold color transitions (e.g., turning red on low health or blue on high charge), inline numeric labels (ShowValue = true), custom tooltips, and interactive drag scrubbing (IsEditable = true).

The Problem: Monitoring vital gauges (player health, boss stamina, battery charge, build progress, cooldown timers) during play testing requires switching focus between the Game View, custom UI overlays, and crowded Inspector panels. Without in-hierarchy bars, debugging multi-entity gameplay states (e.g. 20 spawned enemy health bars) is cumbersome.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [HierarchyProgressBar]:

  • At-a-Glance Telemetry: Monitor health, mana, stamina, and cooldown bars for all active scene entities at once.
  • Dynamic Reflection Bounds: Dynamically query component fields, properties, or methods for maximum and minimum values (e.g. maxHealth).
  • 3-Phase Threshold Colors: Transitions color automatically when entering critical low thresholds (LowThresholdColor) or peak high thresholds (HighThresholdColor).
  • Direct Interactive Scrubbing: Click or drag directly on the hierarchy bar to modify the field value in real time.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class HealthComponent : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyProgressBar(100f, "#10B981", LowThreshold = 0.25f, LowThresholdColor = "#EF4444")]
    public float currentHealth = 75f;
}

  • fillColor: Normal hex color of the filled portion of the bar.

  • fillColor: Normal hex color of the filled portion of the bar.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyPropertyPath]

[HierarchyPropertyPath]

Evaluates and displays nested member paths on the hierarchy row with safe null propagation and custom badge styling.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyPropertyPath] traverses dotted member paths (e.g. stats.currentHp, profile.equipment.weapon.damage) and renders the resolved value inside a styled badge directly on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyPropertyPathRenderer, it handles deep reflection with safe null propagation (NullFallbackText = "—"), custom format strings, font weight styling, borders, and rounded corners. It supports AllowMultiple = true, enabling developers to stack multiple nested member readings side-by-side.

The Problem: Modern game architectures favor clean data structures (composition, nested ScriptableObjects, state machines, serializable structs). However, Unity's default Hierarchy window only displays the GameObject's name. To inspect nested state variables (e.g. current stamina inside an AI sub-state object), developers must drill through nested foldouts in the Inspector.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyPropertyPath]:

  • Deep Member Traversal: Access nested sub-fields, properties, and serializable structs via dot notation (e.g. "attributes.stamina").
  • Graceful Null Propagation: Displays a configurable fallback string (e.g. "None" or "—") without throwing NullReferenceException errors.
  • Multiple Stacked Badges: Apply multiple [HierarchyPropertyPath] attributes to display multiple stats simultaneously.
  • Sleek Badge Styling: Customize background badge colors, borders, font size, and text formatting.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[System.Serializable]
public class UnitStats
{
    public int level = 5;
    public float stamina = 85.5f;
}

[HierarchyPropertyPath(Path = "stats.level", Prefix = "LVL ", BadgeColor = "#1E293B", TextColor = "#F59E0B")]
public class CombatUnit : MonoBehaviour
{
    public UnitStats stats = new UnitStats();
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchySeparator]

[HierarchySeparator]

Transforms a hierarchy row into a styled visual section header with background banner, icon, and accent lines.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchySeparator] turns empty organizational GameObjects into full-width section headers, category banners, and dividers in the Unity Hierarchy window. Driven by HierarchySeparatorRenderer, it paints a full-width background color (BackgroundColor = "#1A1A2E"), an optional bottom accent line (AccentColor = "#7B61FF"), a left edge vertical accent stripe, and built-in icons. It supports customizable text alignment (Left, Center, Right), text overrides, bold/italic typography, and custom font sizes.

The Problem: Large Unity scenes inevitably end up with hundreds of objects. To create visual structure, developers traditionally create dummy GameObjects named --- LIGHTING --- or // ENVIRONMENT //. These pseudo-dividers look messy, blend into the background, and provide no true visual contrast or organization.

The Solution: Decorating header GameObjects with [HierarchySeparator]:

  • Full-Width Section Banners: Renders vibrant, themed background banners across the entire hierarchy row.
  • Accents & Edge Stripes: Features customizable bottom underline borders and left-edge color bars for crisp category delineation.
  • Flexible Text Alignment: Center, left-align, or right-align section labels (HierarchySeparatorTextAlignment).
  • Icon Support: Display native Unity icons or symbols beside the header text.
  • Zero Runtime Overhead: Operates purely in the editor GUI without requiring heavy MonoBehaviour logic or performance penalties.

2. Quick Start

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyStatusDot]

[HierarchyStatusDot]

Draws an interactive colored status indicator dot on the hierarchy row driven by boolean states, numeric thresholds, or static tints.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStatusDot] renders a crisp status indicator dot (or ring) directly on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyStatusDotRenderer, it adapts intelligently to its target:

  • Class-Level: Displays a fixed static status tint (StaticColor = "#888888"), ideal for categorizing subsystems or tagging entity types.
  • Boolean Field: Toggles dynamically between active (TrueColor = "#2ECC71") and inactive states (FalseColor = "#E74C3C").
  • Numeric Field: Evaluates 3-tier thresholds (GreenThreshold, YellowThreshold, MaxValue), transitioning smoothly between Green, Yellow, and Red alerts.
  • Pulsing Animation: Features optional live pulsing when in critical or alert states (PulseWhenCritical = true).

The Problem: Identifying whether an entity is currently online, connected, loaded, or in a critical state (e.g. low energy, dead, disarmed) requires selecting the object and scanning the Inspector. When managing scores of networked entities, drones, or AI bots, developers need instant visual cues to detect failing or inactive systems immediately.

The Solution: Decorating classes or fields with [HierarchyStatusDot]:

  • At-a-Glance State Visibility: Monitor operational health across dozens of entities without clicking into inspectors.
  • Multi-Mode Flexibility: Works with static class tags, boolean switches, and numeric thresholds.
  • Solid or Ring Styles: Render as a solid filled circle or a sleek outlined ring (Outline = true).
  • Critical Pulse Alerts: Subtly pulses dot opacity to grab developer attention during errors or low health states.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class NetworkPeer : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyStatusDot(TrueColor = "#10B981", FalseColor = "#EF4444", PulseWhenCritical = true)]
    public bool isConnected;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyStringField]

[HierarchyStringField]

Draws an inline editable text field directly on the hierarchy row for live string editing without selecting the GameObject.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStringField] binds an inline text input box directly onto the hierarchy row for string fields. Driven by HierarchyStringFieldRenderer, it uses a unique control ID per component instance to maintain keyboard focus, displays dimmed placeholder text when empty (PlaceholderText = "..."), enforces character length caps (MaxLength), and applies sleek dark container styling. In Edit Mode, edits register with Unity's Undo system (Undo.RecordObject) upon change detection; in Play Mode, string updates apply immediately to live component state.

The Problem: Renaming character titles, dialogue identifiers, localization keys, waypoint handles, or notes typically requires clicking the GameObject, waiting for the Inspector to load, finding the string field, editing it, and deselecting. When modifying dozens of dialogue nodes or spawn waypoints, this back-and-forth disrupts creative flow.

The Solution: Decorating string fields with [HierarchyStringField]:

  • Direct Inline Typing: Edit names, tags, and identifiers right in the hierarchy row without selecting the GameObject.
  • Focus Management: Keeps typing focus cleanly isolated per row, preventing accidental GameObject renaming.
  • Placeholder Styling: Shows clean placeholder cues when values are empty.
  • Full Undo Registration: Supports standard Ctrl+Z rollbacks during Edit Mode editing sessions.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyTagSelector]

[HierarchyTagSelector]

Draws an interactive tag selector dropdown directly on the hierarchy row to modify GameObject tags with one click.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTagSelector] attaches to MonoBehaviour classes and renders a clickable tag badge directly on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyTagSelectorRenderer, clicking the badge opens a native Unity GenericMenu populated with all project tags defined in UnityEditorInternal.InternalEditorUtility.tags. Selecting a new tag assigns go.tag, logs full Unity Undo operations (Undo.RegisterCompleteObjectUndo), and marks the scene dirty. It supports auto-hiding for untagged objects (HideUntagged = true) and fully customizable badge colors and borders.

The Problem: Tagging GameObjects (e.g. Player, Enemy, MainCamera, Finish, Hazard) is essential for collision detection, AI perception, and camera framing. However, Unity requires selecting each object, moving to the Inspector, clicking the Tag dropdown, selecting the item, and repeating for every entity. Untagged mistakes are common and hard to spot until gameplay logic fails.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyTagSelector]:

  • Instant Dropdown Picker: Click directly on the hierarchy row to open a full popup menu of all project tags.
  • Fast Scene Auditing: Immediately see which tag is assigned to each entity without selecting it.
  • Optional Clean Defaults: Hide the badge when the object is Untagged to keep standard objects visual noise-free.
  • Full Undo Registration: Supports standard Ctrl+Z rollbacks during Edit Mode editing sessions.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[HierarchyToggle]

[HierarchyToggle]

Draws an interactive checkbox toggle directly on the hierarchy row bound to a boolean field.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyToggle] binds an interactive toggle checkbox directly to a serialized boolean field on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyToggleRenderer, clicking the checkbox flips the boolean value immediately. It supports optional inline text labels (Label), custom active state tinting (ActiveTint), custom tooltips, and complete Undo integration (Undo.RecordObject) in Edit Mode while executing seamlessly in Play Mode.

The Problem: During level design, testing, and debugging, developers frequently need to flip boolean switches (e.g., enableDebugGizmos, isInvulnerable, autoSpawn, muteSFX, drawNavMeshPath). Opening each GameObject's Inspector to toggle a single boolean takes multiple clicks and breaks debugging focus.

The Solution: Decorating boolean fields with [HierarchyToggle]:

  • Single-Click In-Hierarchy Toggling: Toggle boolean fields on and off with a single click right in the hierarchy row.
  • Accompanying Label Support: Display a short identifier label (e.g. "AI", "Debug", "God") beside the checkbox.
  • Visual State Feedback: Clearly see whether the flag is active without selecting the entity.
  • Undo Integration: Records full Undo history in Edit Mode to ensure accidental toggles can be undone instantly.

Hierarchy Designer/Core Hierarchy/[Hierarchy]

[Hierarchy]

Foundational base attribute for all Unity Hierarchy window decorators, interactive widgets, badges, and telemetry overlays.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [Hierarchy] is the root architectural attribute powering the entire MSUltimate-HierarchyDesigner ecosystem. Decorator classes deriving from HierarchyAttribute hook directly into the Unity Editor's hierarchyWindowItemOnGUI pipeline via the high-performance HierarchyRendererEngine. The base attribute provides core lifecycle controls common to all hierarchy decorators: whether the decorator renders during active Play Mode (IsActiveInPlayMode = true), and the precise horizontal/drawing evaluation order when stacking multiple decorators on a single GameObject (Order = 0).

The Problem: Standard Unity inspectors isolate component data strictly inside the Inspector window. When navigating complex game scenes with hundreds of nested entities, developers must click every individual GameObject one by one to inspect their component types, active states, health percentages, or layer assignments.

The Solution: Extending and utilizing [Hierarchy]:

  • Direct Hierarchy Visualization: Surfaces crucial runtime variables, alerts, and interactive buttons straight into the Hierarchy list view.
  • Unified Draw Order Architecture: The Order property allows developers to arrange multiple decorators from left to right with deterministic priority.
  • PlayMode Lifecycle Control: Toggle IsActiveInPlayMode to hide diagnostic clutter during gameplay testing or keep live debug telemetry active.
  • High-Performance Caching: The underlying renderer engine caches component reflection metadata, preventing GC allocations during rapid editor repaints.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

// Demonstrating standard derived usage of the Hierarchy attribute family
[HierarchyHighlight("#2E4057", Order = 0)]
[HierarchyActiveButton(Order = 1)]
public class SpawnPointEntity : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string spawnZoneTag = "Sector-Alpha";
    public int teamAffiliation = 1;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyAudioMonitor]

[HierarchyAudioMonitor]

Displays an animated VU volume meter and interactive play/mute toggle button in the hierarchy for AudioSource components.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyAudioMonitor] embeds live audio telemetry and control directly into the hierarchy row for GameObjects with AudioSource components. Driven by HierarchyAudioMonitorRenderer, it displays 3 live animated VU meter equalizer bars (MeterColor = "#2ED573") whenever audio is actively playing. When paused or muted, it switches to a clean status icon ("♫" or "✕" in MutedColor = "#747D8C"). Clicking the button immediately toggles audio.mute with full Unity Undo registration in Edit Mode and instant muting during Play Mode.

The Problem: Diagnosing audio issues (e.g., sound effects failing to play, runaway audio sources blowing out the master mix, looping ambient sounds playing in the wrong scene) often requires opening the Audio Profiler window or clicking through multiple AudioSource objects in the hierarchy to check playback flags and volume sliders.

The Solution: Decorating audio managers or sound triggers with [HierarchyAudioMonitor]:

  • Animated Equalizer Feedback: Instantly see which AudioSources in the scene are actively emitting sound via animated VU meter bars.
  • One-Click Muting: Click the hierarchy widget to instantly mute or unmute specific audio sources without deselecting current objects.
  • Full Undo Support: Audio mute toggles record to the Unity Undo stack (Undo.RecordObject).
  • Play Mode & Edit Mode Support: Visualizes audio previews during both editor testing and runtime gameplay.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyComponentIcons]

[HierarchyComponentIcons]

Renders a horizontal strip of mini component icons directly on the hierarchy row with type tooltips.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyComponentIcons] scans all components attached to a GameObject and renders a compact horizontal strip of mini native Unity component icons (IconSize = 12f, Spacing = 2f) on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyComponentIconsRenderer, it queries EditorGUIUtility.ObjectContent(comp, comp.GetType()) to resolve accurate Unity editor icons (e.g., BoxCollider, Rigidbody, NavMeshAgent, Light, AudioSource, Animator), ignoring redundant Transform components and capping display count at MaxIcons (default 5). Hovering over any icon reveals a tooltip showing the exact component class name.

The Problem: In dense scene hierarchies, determining what components are attached to a GameObject requires selecting each entity one by one and scrolling through the Inspector. Developers frequently misplace colliders, miss unassigned animators, or forget whether an object has a Rigidbody, resulting in tedious manual audits.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyComponentIcons]:

  • Immediate Architectural Inspection: See all functional components attached to an entity at a glance in the Hierarchy.
  • Accurate Unity Native Iconography: Uses official icons matched by Unity EditorGUIUtility for instant visual recognition.
  • Hover Inspection Tooltips: Hover over any icon to verify the exact type name without opening foldouts.
  • Configurable Caps & Spacing: Keep hierarchy rows clean and uncluttered by bounding icon count (MaxIcons) and adjusting padding.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyDrawCallTracker]

[HierarchyDrawCallTracker]

Analyzes submesh and material counts across renderers, flagging potential batch-breaking setups with an alert badge.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyDrawCallTracker] inspects the GameObject and all of its child renderers (Renderer.sharedMaterials) to identify how many unique materials are assigned. Driven by HierarchyDrawCallTrackerRenderer, it renders a compact badge directly on the hierarchy row displaying the unique material count (e.g., "2 Mat"). When the count exceeds MaxAllowedMaterials (default 2), it switches from a neutral gray badge (NormalColor = "#57606F") to a vibrant alert color (AlertColor = "#FFA502").

The Problem: In 3D game development, excessive material assignments are the number one cause of broken SRP batching, high draw call counts, and degraded GPU frame rates. Artists and level designers often combine models with multiple submeshes and independent materials without realizing that each unique material introduces separate draw calls.

The Solution: Decorating environment props and character rigs with [HierarchyDrawCallTracker]:

  • Immediate Batching Auditing: See the exact material footprint directly in the Hierarchy window.
  • Automated High-Material Alerts: Visually flags objects that break batching thresholds (AlertColor).
  • Child Subtree Aggregation: Uses GetComponentsInChildren<Renderer>(true) to gather all mesh renderers and skinned meshes in the subtree.
  • Zero Runtime Draw Calls: Runs purely within editor GUI rendering without allocating game materials or mutating instances.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyDrawCallTracker(MaxAllowedMaterials = 2, AlertColor = "#EF4444")]
public class ModularEnvironmentPiece : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string assetId = "WALL_MODULAR_A";
}

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyEventHookTracker]

[HierarchyEventHookTracker]

Audits UnityEvent listener counts on the hierarchy row, flagging empty or broken callback hooks with rich tooltips.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyEventHookTracker] inspects serialized UnityEvent fields on the GameObject and displays a diagnostic listener badge directly on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyEventHookTrackerRenderer, it audits every persistent listener hook:

  • Valid Connections: Displays the active listener count in green (ValidColor = "#27AE60") (e.g. "[2 evt]").
  • Broken / Missing Targets: Immediately turns red (BrokenColor = "#C0392B") and alerts with "❌ broken" if any listener target object is null or its bound method is missing.
  • Empty Listeners: Alerts with orange/amber badge (EmptyColor = "#D35400") when no callbacks are wired (WarnIfEmpty = true).
  • Rich Diagnostic Tooltips: Hovering over the badge displays a multi-line breakdown of every connected target and method name (e.g., ✔ AudioManager.PlaySfx(), ❌ MISSING TARGET / METHOD).

The Problem: Broken UnityEvents are notorious causes of silent bugs in gameplay logic (UI buttons that don't trigger events, triggers that don't open doors, health callbacks that fail to report deaths). Because broken events do not throw compile-time errors, developers only discover them during manual play sessions.

The Solution: Decorating event-driven components with [HierarchyEventHookTracker]:

  • Immediate Broken Event Detection: Catch missing targets and renamed method references directly in the Hierarchy.
  • Empty Callback Warnings: Identify unhooked events before shipping or testing scenes.
  • Class or Field Targeting: Can be placed directly on a UnityEvent field or on the class declaring EventFieldName.
  • Multi-Event Stacking: Apply multiple attributes to monitor distinct events (e.g., onClicked, onHover, onCancel).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using UnityEngine.Events;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class InteractionTrigger : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyEventHookTracker]
    public UnityEvent onInteracted;
}

public class DynamicCampfireLight : MonoBehaviour
{
    private Light _light;

    [Header("Flicker Configuration")]
    public float baseIntensity = 3.0f;
    public float flickerRange = 0.8f;

    private void Awake()
    {
        _light = GetComponent<Light>();
    }

    private void Update()
    {
        if (_light != null)
        {
            _light.intensity = baseIntensity + Mathf.Sin(Time.time * 8f) * flickerRange;
        }
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyMemoryTracker]

[HierarchyMemoryTracker]

Displays estimated RAM and VRAM asset footprint on the hierarchy row with threshold-based alert badges.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyMemoryTracker] calculates the real-time runtime memory footprint of all assets referenced by a GameObject (Meshes, Textures, Sprites, and AudioClips) and displays the formatted size directly on the hierarchy row (e.g. "💾 1.4 MB" or "💾 250 KB"). Driven by HierarchyMemoryTrackerRenderer, it queries Unity's Profiler.GetRuntimeMemorySizeLong and applies a 3-tier color system:

  • Normal: Displays in green (NormalColor = "#27AE60") when below warning thresholds.
  • Warning: Transitions to amber (WarningColor = "#F39C12") when exceeding WarningThresholdMB (default 2.0 MB).
  • Critical: Flashes red (CriticalColor = "#C0392B") when exceeding CriticalThresholdMB (default 8.0 MB).
  • Child Subtree Aggregation: Can optionally aggregate memory across all child GameObjects (IncludeChildren = true).

The Problem: High-resolution 4K textures, uncompressed audio clips, and massive high-poly meshes frequently cause unexpected memory bloat, mobile crashes, and frame stutters during asset streaming. Finding which specific GameObject or prefab variant is consuming memory usually requires running a full memory capture in the Unity Memory Profiler, which takes time and cannot be monitored while building scenes.

The Solution: Decorating components or prefabs with [HierarchyMemoryTracker]:

  • Real-Time Memory Telemetry: See the exact asset memory consumption right beside the object name in the Hierarchy.
  • 3-Tier Threshold Alerts: Instantly spot bloated assets that violate memory budgets for mobile, VR, or consoles.
  • Subtree Memory Aggregation: Sums up child assets to measure total prefab or environment cluster footprints.
  • Detailed Byte Tooltips: Hovering over the badge displays exact byte counts alongside megabyte readouts.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyMemoryTracker(WarningThresholdMB = 4.0f, CriticalThresholdMB = 12.0f)]
public class HighResHeroCharacter : MonoBehaviour
{
    public Mesh heroMesh;
    public Texture2D diffuseMap;
    public Texture2D normalMap;
}

PackageMSUltimate-HierarchyDesigner Addon

InheritedYes
Allow MultipleNo (AllowMultiple = false)

Render EngineHierarchyMemoryTrackerRenderer (IMGUI)

  • Standard parameterless constructor. Customizations are configured via object initializer properties.

Hierarchy Designer/Diagnostics & Performance/[HierarchyPolyCount]

[HierarchyPolyCount]

Inspects mesh renderers and displays vertex or triangle counts directly on the hierarchy row with high-poly alert thresholds.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyPolyCount] calculates polygon or vertex counts for GameObjects equipped with MeshFilter or SkinnedMeshRenderer components and renders a compact telemetry badge directly on the hierarchy row (e.g. "🔺 14.2k", "🔺 850"). Driven by HierarchyPolyCountRenderer, it supports toggling between triangle counts (ShowTriangles = true) and vertex counts (ShowTriangles = false), aggregating counts across all child GameObjects (IncludeChildren = true), and switching to a distinct warning color (WarnColor = "#FF4757") whenever counts exceed WarnThreshold (default 15,000).

The Problem: Unoptimized 3D models with runaway triangle counts (un-decimated CAD imports, high-density photogrammetry assets, excessive hair/fur geometries) severely degrade rasterization performance and push mobile or VR projects over tight vertex budgets. Identifying high-poly culled meshes usually requires selecting each mesh and inspecting the Asset Importer or opening the Scene Statistics overlay, which only shows full-scene aggregates.

The Solution: Decorating 3D models and prefab roots with [HierarchyPolyCount]:

  • Direct Poly Telemetry: See exact triangle or vertex counts right in the hierarchy row without selecting the model.
  • Subtree Aggregation: Sums up child mesh renderers to evaluate complete complex character or environment prefabs.
  • Budget Violation Alerts: Turns vibrant red when exceeding defined polygon budgets (WarnThreshold).
  • Formatted Readouts: Uses compact formatting (e.g., 12.4k, 1.2M) with exact counts displayed in hover tooltips.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyPolyCount(WarnThreshold = 20000, NormalColor = "#38BDF8", WarnColor = "#EF4444")]
public class PropMeshRoot : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string modelVariant = "CASTLE_TURRET_LOD0";
}

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchyColorSwatch]

[HierarchyColorSwatch]

Displays an interactive color swatch square that opens Unity's native Color Picker directly from the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyColorSwatch] binds an interactive color preview square (Size = 12f) directly onto the hierarchy row for Color or Color32 fields or properties. Driven by HierarchyColorSwatchRenderer, it uses EditorGUI.ColorField to allow single-click opening of Unity's native Color Picker. Modifying the color records full Unity Undo history (Undo.RecordObject) in Edit Mode, marks components dirty, and prevents accidental GameObject selection or name renaming.

The Problem: Tuning visual colors across scene lights, particle effects, UI themes, environment props, or debug markers requires repeatedly clicking GameObjects, waiting for the Inspector to load, finding the color property, tweaking it, and repeating for adjacent objects.

The Solution: Decorating color fields or properties with [HierarchyColorSwatch]:

  • Direct Color Tweaking: Open Unity's Color Picker straight from the hierarchy row with a single click.
  • Instant Visual Identification: Color swatches immediately show current team colors, lighting tints, or material color states.
  • Alpha Channel Support: Option to include or omit the alpha channel (ShowAlpha).
  • Supports Color and Color32: Automatically handles both standard floating-point UnityEngine.Color and byte-based UnityEngine.Color32 types.
  • Full Undo Registration: Supports standard Ctrl+Z rollbacks during Edit Mode editing sessions.

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchyLock]

[HierarchyLock]

Renders an interactive lock icon directly on the hierarchy row to toggle edit protection and scene picking locks.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyLock] adds an interactive padlock icon button (Size = 14f) to the hierarchy row of GameObjects. Driven by HierarchyLockRenderer, clicking the lock icon toggles HideFlags.NotEditable on the GameObject. When locked, the icon illuminates in gold (LockedColor = "#FFD700", LockIcon-On), preventing accidental transform moves, component deletion, or inspector edits. When unlocked, it renders in dim slate (UnlockedColor = "#576574", LockIcon). It optionally cascades lock states recursively down through the entire child hierarchy (AffectChildren = true).

The Problem: In production level design, finalized environmental assets, terrain chunks, cinematic cameras, and static lighting rigs should not be moved or edited accidentally. Unity's standard lock is hidden inside inspector menus or scene picking flags, making it easy for artists or designers to accidentally nudge or modify finalized props during selection.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyLock]:

  • Single-Click Lock Protection: Lock or unlock any entity with one click right in the hierarchy row.
  • Visual Padlock Feedback: Vibrant gold padlock icon clearly distinguishes locked entities from editable objects.
  • Recursive Child Locking: Protect entire prefab hierarchies and environment branches simultaneously (AffectChildren = true).
  • Complete Undo Integration: Every lock/unlock action registers with Unity's undo manager (Undo.RecordObject).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyLock(LockedColor = "#F59E0B", AffectChildren = true)]
public class StaticEnvironmentAsset : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string assetSignature = "ENVIRONMENT_TERRAIN_A";
}

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchyMethodButton]

[HierarchyMethodButton]

Renders a clickable action button directly on the hierarchy row to invoke parameterless component methods with one click.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyMethodButton] attaches to parameterless methods on MonoBehaviour components and embeds a clickable button directly on the right edge of the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyMethodButtonRenderer, clicking the button invokes the method via reflection, records full Unity Undo operations (Undo.RecordObject), and marks the component dirty. It supports custom button labels (Text), color themes (ButtonColor, TextColor), auto-sizing or fixed widths, and custom horizontal placement.

The Problem: Triggering utility or debug actions (e.g., ResetPosition(), RespawnEnemies(), BakeLightingProbe(), ClearInventory(), TestAttack()) usually requires selecting the GameObject, scrolling past dozens of inspector fields to find a custom button or context menu, and clicking it. When testing frequently, this workflow is repetitive and slow.

The Solution: Decorating methods with [HierarchyMethodButton]:

  • Single-Click In-Hierarchy Execution: Run utility, debug, or editor actions directly from the hierarchy row.
  • Visual Button Customization: Customize button labels, colors, and sizing for clear differentiation.
  • Full Undo Registration: Integrates with Unity's undo manager in Edit Mode, ensuring state changes can be rolled back with Ctrl+Z.
  • Zero Runtime Overhead: Buttons exist solely within the Editor GUI lifecycle and do not impact standalone builds.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class SpawnerController : MonoBehaviour
{
    public GameObject enemyPrefab;

    [HierarchyMethodButton(Text = "Spawn Wave", ButtonColor = "#3B82F6")]
    public void TriggerWave()
    {
        Debug.Log("Spawning new enemy wave!");
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchyMiniSlider]

[HierarchyMiniSlider]

Renders a compact draggable horizontal slider directly on the hierarchy row for live numeric tuning.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyMiniSlider] binds a compact, interactive slider directly to numeric fields or properties on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyMiniSliderRenderer, dragging the slider bar updates the numeric value in real time. It uses Unity's GUIUtility.hotControl mechanism to capture mouse drag interactions smoothly without conflicting with hierarchy row dragging or selection. It records full Unity Undo history in Edit Mode (Undo.RecordObject) and mutates fields directly during Play Mode.

The Problem: Balancing scalar properties (e.g., audio volume, light intensity, AI aggro radius, camera FOV, spawn probabilities) requires selecting each entity, locating its slider in the Inspector, scrubbing, and checking results in the Scene or Game View. Toggling back and forth between multiple GameObjects to balance their relative values is cumbersome.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields or properties with [HierarchyMiniSlider]:

  • Direct Hierarchy Scrubbing: Click and drag the slider track right in the hierarchy row to tune values instantly.
  • Custom Value Range: Set explicit MinValue and MaxValue boundaries (e.g., 0f to 1f or 0f to 100f).
  • Stylized Visual Bar: Features custom fill and background colors with smooth rounded edges.
  • Conflict-Free Dragging: Captures mouse drag focus so dragging does not accidentally reorder hierarchy nodes.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchySceneViewPing]

[HierarchySceneViewPing]

Adds a navigation crosshair button directly on the hierarchy row to instantly focus and frame the Scene View camera on the object.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchySceneViewPing] places an interactive crosshair navigation icon on the hierarchy row of high-value GameObjects. Driven by HierarchySceneViewPingRenderer:

  • Single-Click Framing: Instantly calculates the composite bounding box across all child Renderers and Colliders, then smoothly frames the active Scene View camera (SceneView.lastActiveSceneView.Frame(bounds, false)) onto the object.
  • Shift+Click Ping: Holding Shift while clicking immediately triggers EditorGUIUtility.PingObject(go), highlighting the entity in the Hierarchy without moving the Scene camera.
  • Custom Iconography & Tints: Renders using Unity's native d_ViewToolMove icon with customizable color tinting (IconColor = "#00E5FF").

The Problem: In expansive 3D open worlds or large multi-room levels, navigating to specific spawn points, trigger volumes, boss arenas, or collectible items requires selecting the object in the Hierarchy, moving your cursor over to the Scene View, and pressing F to frame. If the Scene View is not active, framing fails. Repeating this dozens of times during level design is tedious.

The Solution: Decorating key components with [HierarchySceneViewPing]:

  • One-Click Camera Navigation: Instantly frame key scene entities with a single click right on the hierarchy row.
  • Composite Bounds Calculation: Automatically encapsulates child renderers and colliders so the camera zooms to the perfect framing distance.
  • Dual Framing / Ping Modes: Frame on regular click; ping on Shift+Click.
  • Visual Crosshair Cue: Distinct cyan crosshair makes key gameplay landmarks stand out in the hierarchy.

2. Quick Start

csharp

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

{
    public int spawnIndex = 0;

Hierarchy Designer/Interactions & Tools/[HierarchySolo]

[HierarchySolo]

Renders a solo toggle button directly on the hierarchy row to isolate the entity by hiding scene siblings and restoring them on toggle.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchySolo] adds a DAW-style "Solo" toggle button ("S") directly on the hierarchy row of GameObjects. Driven by HierarchySoloRenderer, clicking the button isolates the selected GameObject by temporarily deactivating all of its sibling GameObjects. It caches the original active states of all siblings in an internal session dictionary. Clicking the button a second time toggles solo mode off, instantly restoring every sibling back to its exact prior active/inactive state.

The Problem: When working on lighting, VFX, physics prefabs, or complex environment clusters, surrounding objects clutter the Scene View and occlude the entity being tweaked. Manually turning off 20 sibling objects, tweaking the target, and then remembering which siblings were originally active and which were inactive is tedious and error-prone.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchySolo]:

  • Single-Click Solo Isolation: Instantly isolate any GameObject from its siblings with a single click in the hierarchy.
  • Accurate State Restoration: Caches the original state of each sibling, ensuring deactivated debug props remain inactive when solo mode ends.
  • Visual Yellow Active State: Active solo buttons glow in vibrant yellow (ActiveColor = "#FFD32A"), making isolated objects unmistakable.
  • Root or Child Scope: Works cleanly on root scene entities or nested children inside complex prefab hierarchies.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchySolo]
public class LightingHeroFixture : MonoBehaviour
{
    public Light keyLight;
    public Light fillLight;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Styling & VFX/[HierarchyBannerSkin]

[HierarchyBannerSkin]

Renders a themed skinned header banner row across the hierarchy for styled visual grouping.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBannerSkin] transforms GameObjects into themed section headers and dividers directly in the Unity Hierarchy window. Driven by HierarchyBannerSkinRenderer, it paints a full-width themed background rect, custom accent border stripes on the left and bottom edges, and theme-specific icon prefixes (e.g. "◈ ", "⚔ ", "⚠ ", "◼ ", "● "). It features 5 built-in aesthetic presets via the HierarchyBannerTheme enum (SciFiCyber, FantasyParchment, CarbonDark, MinimalistClean, HazardIndustrial), with full support for custom title strings and accent color overrides.

The Problem: Standard Unity hierarchies lack visual personality and aesthetic organization. Game developers often create plain divider objects with text like --- CAMERAS ---, which blend into standard scene objects and provide no distinctive styling or theme cohesion.

The Solution: Decorating header GameObjects with [HierarchyBannerSkin]:

  • Themed Visual Headers: Choose from 5 curated design aesthetics matching your game's genre (Sci-Fi, Fantasy, Industrial, Minimal, Carbon).
  • Edge Accent Stripes: Renders crisp vertical accent bars on the left margin and thin underlines on the bottom edge.
  • Custom Accent Tinting: Override accent colors (AccentColor = "#00FFAA") to match project color palettes.
  • Title Override Support: Specify a custom title or let it automatically format the GameObject's name in uppercase.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyBannerSkin(Theme = HierarchyBannerTheme.SciFiCyber, Title = "AI DIRECTORS", AccentColor = "#00E5FF")]
public class AiSectionBanner : MonoBehaviour
{
}

csharp
using UnityEngine;

    Theme = HierarchyBannerTheme.FantasyParchment, 
    Title = "DUNGEON BOSS RIGS", 
    AccentColor = "#F59E0B")]
public class BossSectionDivider : MonoBehaviour
{
}
