Hierarchy Designer/Styling & VFX/[HierarchyCooldownRing]

[HierarchyCooldownRing]

Displays a circular radial cooldown ring directly on the hierarchy row for timed abilities, recharge meters, or weapon timers.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyCooldownRing] renders a sleek circular radial progress ring directly on the hierarchy row for numeric cooldown or timer fields. Driven by HierarchyCooldownRingRenderer, it calculates the elapsed ratio (current / MaxDuration) and draws a background guide ring alongside a smoothly sweeping active radial arc (DrawRadialArc). It supports customizable ring colors (RingColor = "#FF9F43"), background track tints (BackgroundColor = "#341F97"), radius sizes, and horizontal offsets.

The Problem: Watching cooldown timers, ability recharges, spawn delays, or spell cycle times during gameplay sessions usually requires watching in-game UI canvases or reading raw float values inside the Inspector. When balancing 10 different spells or weapon cooldowns simultaneously, developers need glanceable circular ring telemetry in the hierarchy.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields or properties with [HierarchyCooldownRing]:

  • Radial Sweeping Gauge: Visualizes cooldown progress as a smooth radial arc sweeping from 0° to 360°.
  • Compact Hierarchy Footprint: Uses a tiny radius (Radius = 6f) that fits neatly alongside object names without cluttering rows.
  • Configurable Durations: Set fixed maximum durations (MaxDuration = 10f) to normalize progress calculation.
  • Play Mode & Edit Mode Support: Updates smoothly every frame during active gameplay or editor testing.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class SpellCaster : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyCooldownRing(MaxDuration = 5.0f, RingColor = "#38BDF8")]
    public float fireballCooldown = 3.2f;
}

    public float reloadTimeSeconds = 4.5f;

        MaxDuration = 4.5f, 
        RingColor = "#F97316", 
        BackgroundColor = "#1E293B", 
        Radius = 7f, 
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public float currentReloadTimer = 2.1f;

    public int ammoCount = 30;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Styling & VFX/[HierarchyGlassmorphism]

[HierarchyGlassmorphism]

Renders a frosted glass translucent background with a specular rim highlight across the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyGlassmorphism] styles the hierarchy row with modern frosted glass aesthetics (glassmorphism). Driven by HierarchyGlassmorphismRenderer, it utilizes HierarchyDrawUtilities.DrawGlassmorphism to paint a smooth translucent background card tint (BlurColor = "#1E2A38"), an ultra-fine specular rim highlight line across the top edge (SpecularOpacity = 0.5f), and a subtle translucent border outline (BorderAlpha = 0.2f).

The Problem: Standard hierarchy rows in Unity use flat gray backgrounds. Customizing rows with harsh solid color blocks can feel jarring and visually overwhelming. Developers want a premium, modern AAA look that makes key entities stand out without overpowering surrounding text or icons.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyGlassmorphism]:

  • Frosted Glass Panel Aesthetics: Applies a sleek, translucent modern card styling directly over the hierarchy row.
  • Top Specular Rim Light: A subtle 1px white highlight line gives the impression of refractive glass.
  • Custom Blur Tint & Alpha: Fine-tune background hue, specular brightness, and border alpha.
  • Non-Intrusive Contrast: Hierarchy text, badges, and icons remain crisp and legible against the frosted panel.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyGlassmorphism]
public class MainPlayerRig : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float movementSpeed = 6.5f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Styling & VFX/[HierarchyGlow]

[HierarchyGlow]

Renders an ambient glow halo or pulsating neon border highlighting high-priority entities in the hierarchy.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyGlow] surrounds high-priority GameObjects with an atmospheric, glowing neon border or halo directly within the Hierarchy window. Driven by HierarchyGlowRenderer, it utilizes HierarchyDrawUtilities.DrawGlowHalo to project a soft, feathered ambient border (GlowColor = "#9B59B6"). It supports live sinusoidal pulsation (Pulse = true) with customizable speed (PulseSpeed = 2.0f) and intensity multipliers (Intensity = 1.0f), creating an unmissable beacon for mission-critical objects.

The Problem: In large hierarchies with hundreds of objects, finding the active Player controller, the level exit trigger, a vital singleton manager, or a critical bug-tracking target requires scanning text line by line. Standard highlight colors can look flat or blend into regular selections.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyGlow]:

  • Ambient Neon Halo: Surrounds the row with a feathered, glowing border that immediately draws developer focus.
  • Pulsating Beacon Effect: Smoothly pulses in opacity over time using sinusoidal real-time math.
  • Fully Customizable Styling: Configure glow color, halo intensity, pulse state, and frequency.
  • Class or Field Scope: Apply at the class level for whole-row glowing or on specific fields.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyGlow(GlowColor = "#00FFAA", Intensity = 1.2f, Pulse = true)]
public class QuestObjectiveTarget : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string objectiveId = "FIND_RELIC";
}

    public GameObject bossPrefab;
    public bool isEncounterTriggered = false;

Hierarchy Designer/Styling & VFX/[HierarchyStateIndicator]

[HierarchyStateIndicator]

Displays finite state machine status with transition pills and directional arrows directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStateIndicator] displays active state machine or enum values directly on the hierarchy row formatted with a directional arrow badge (➔ State). Driven by HierarchyStateIndicatorRenderer, it reads current state values from serialized fields or properties (raw.ToString()), wraps them in a rounded pill badge (ActiveBadgeColor = "#2ED573"), and renders crisp white state labels (TextColor = "#FFFFFF").

The Problem: Monitoring AI states (e.g., Idle, Patrol, Chase, Attack, Flee), player movement states (Grounded, Airborne, Sliding), or cutscene stages during play testing requires selecting each entity and inspecting internal state machine foldouts. When testing group behaviors (e.g. pack AI coordination), watching 8 entities simultaneously in the inspector is impossible.

The Solution: Decorating state fields or properties with [HierarchyStateIndicator]:

  • Live State Machine Feedback: Instantly see what state each AI or player entity is executing right in the hierarchy row.
  • Directional Arrow Cue: Formats text with an intuitive directional prefix (e.g. ➔ Patrol, ➔ Attack).
  • Rounded Pill Container: Auto-sizes around the text and applies custom background badge styling.
  • Supports Enums and Strings: Evaluates any enum, string, or class state object via clean .ToString() resolution.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public enum EnemyState { Idle, Patrol, Chase, Attack }

public class EnemyBrain : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyStateIndicator(ActiveBadgeColor = "#6366F1")]
    public EnemyState currentState = EnemyState.Patrol;
}

    public float phaseTimer = 0f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyBitmask]

[HierarchyBitmask]

Displays bitmask or flags enum fields as a compact row of LED matrix dots directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBitmask] renders an intuitive horizontal matrix of miniature LED status dots directly on the hierarchy row for integer or bitmask flags fields. Driven by HierarchyBitmaskRenderer, it checks each bit up to BitCount (default 8) and paints active high bits with vibrant illuminated colors (ActiveColor = "#00FFAA") and inactive low bits with dim slate dots (InactiveColor = "#34495E").

The Problem: Reading binary flag states, collision layers, capability bits, or active perk masks in Unity requires decoding raw integers (e.g. 23 or 0x17) or opening multi-selection enum dropdowns in the Inspector. When inspecting multiple AI agents or network clients, comparing which entities have specific flags set is tedious.

The Solution: Decorating integer or enum flag fields with [HierarchyBitmask]:

  • Miniature LED Matrix Row: View up to 32 individual bits as a row of illuminated dots right on the hierarchy row.
  • Instant Visual Pattern Recognition: Immediately distinguish bit patterns across entities without mental decimal-to-binary conversion.
  • Configurable Dimensions & Colors: Customize active/inactive colors, dot diameter (DotSize = 4f), and spacing.
  • Zero Overhead in Builds: Runs purely within editor GUI rendering without allocations.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[System.Flags]
public enum EntityCapabilities
{
    CanMove = 1 << 0,
    CanAttack = 1 << 1,
    CanFly = 1 << 2,
    Invulnerable = 1 << 3
}

public class CombatEntity : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyBitmask(BitCount = 4, ActiveColor = "#10B981", InactiveColor = "#334155")]
    public EntityCapabilities capabilities = EntityCapabilities.CanMove | EntityCapabilities.CanAttack;
}

InactiveColorstring"#34495E"Hex color for unset (low / 0) bits.
DotSizefloat4fDiameter in pixels of each LED bit dot.
Spacingfloat2fSpacing in pixels between adjacent LED dots.
XOffsetfloat4fHorizontal offset in pixels from the right margin of the hierarchy row.
IsActiveInPlayModebooltrueWhether the bitmask display remains visible during Play Mode.
Orderint0Drawing order priority relative to other hierarchy row items.

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class NetworkPermissions : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Security Masks")]
    [HierarchyBitmask(
        BitCount = 8, 
        ActiveColor = "#38BDF8", 
        InactiveColor = "#1E293B", 
        DotSize = 5f, 
        Spacing = 2.5f, 
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public byte securityFlags = 0b00101101;

    public int clientId = 1042;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyCurveVisualizer]

[HierarchyCurveVisualizer]

Renders a live mini waveform curve in the hierarchy row for AnimationCurve fields with playhead evaluation.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyCurveVisualizer] renders an anti-aliased micro-waveform curve graph directly on the hierarchy row for serialized AnimationCurve fields. Driven by HierarchyCurveVisualizerRenderer, it samples the curve across 16 anti-aliased points, normalizes min/max amplitudes, and paints the wave within a sleek dark container card (BackgroundColor = "#121A24", BorderColor = "#1E3A50"). During Play Mode, it can evaluate and plot a live moving playhead dot along the curve (TrackPlayhead = true), optionally querying a reflected time field (PlayheadTimeField).

The Problem: AnimationCurves govern vital gameplay mechanics (weapon recoil profiles, easing transitions, light falloffs, jump trajectory parabolas, damage curves). Inspecting or comparing curves across different weapon prefabs or character rigs currently requires opening the Inspector and double-clicking each curve into Unity's popup Curve Editor window.

The Solution: Decorating AnimationCurve fields with [HierarchyCurveVisualizer]:

  • Miniature Curve Waveform: See the curve's profile (ease-in, ease-out, parabola, s-curve) at a glance in the hierarchy.
  • Live Playhead Tracking: Watch real-time evaluation dot movement along the waveform during gameplay.
  • Reflected Time Synchronization: Bind to float time/progress variables via PlayheadTimeField to evaluate actual curve playback.
  • Custom Visual Styling: Customize line color (LineColor = "#55DDFF"), container borders, and width.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class JumpController : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyCurveVisualizer(LineColor = "#38BDF8", Width = 45f)]
    public AnimationCurve jumpTrajectory = AnimationCurve.EaseInOut(0f, 0f, 1f, 1f);
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class WeaponRecoilRig : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Recoil Curve")]
    [HierarchyCurveVisualizer(
        Width = 50f, 
        LineColor = "#F43F5E", 
        BackgroundColor = "#1E293B", 
        BorderColor = "#475569", 
        TrackPlayhead = true, 
        PlayheadTimeField = nameof(recoilProgress), 
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public AnimationCurve recoilImpulse = AnimationCurve.Linear(0f, 0f, 0.2f, 1f);

    [HideInInspector]
    public float recoilProgress = 0f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyDeltaTracker]

[HierarchyDeltaTracker]

Observes and displays real-time rate of change indicators for numeric fields directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyDeltaTracker] measures frame-to-frame numeric fluctuations and displays a live directional rate-of-change indicator on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyDeltaTrackerRenderer, it compares the current value with the previous frame's reading:

  • Increasing Values: Displays an upward arrow in green (PositiveColor = "#2ECC71", e.g. "▲ +1.5").
  • Decreasing Values: Displays a downward arrow in red (NegativeColor = "#E74C3C", e.g. "▼ -0.8").
  • Stable Values: Displays a neutral dot in slate (NeutralColor = "#95A5A6", e.g. "● 0.0").
  • Format Flexibility: Supports custom C# numeric formatting strings (Format = "{0:+0.0;-0.0;0.0}").

The Problem: When balancing dynamic gameplay systems (player acceleration, physics velocities, health regeneration, resource drain rates, heat buildup), watching the absolute value alone does not tell you if the value is currently rising or dropping, or how fast the change is occurring.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields or properties with [HierarchyDeltaTracker]:

  • Instant Directional Rate Feedback: Immediately see whether values are rising, dropping, or plateauing.
  • Color-Coded Status Symbols: Intuitive green ▲, red ▼, and gray ● cues provide instant visual clarity.
  • Custom Numeric Precision: Control floating-point precision to filter out micro-jitter or display high-precision rates.
  • Per-Component History: Tracks each component instance independently in editor session memory.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class PlayerMovement : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyDeltaTracker(PositiveColor = "#10B981", NegativeColor = "#EF4444")]
    public float currentSpeed;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyRadialGauge]

[HierarchyRadialGauge]

Renders a circular donut progress ring for percentages, stamina, heat, or energy on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyRadialGauge] transforms numeric fields into a compact, circular donut progress ring directly on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyRadialGaugeRenderer, it computes the fill ratio between MinValue and MaxValue, rendering a full 360° background guide track (BackgroundColor = "#2C3E50") and an active radial progress arc (RingColor = "#00D2FF", Thickness = 2.5f).

The Problem: Linear horizontal progress bars can consume significant horizontal space on the hierarchy row, competing with long GameObject names or adjacent status icons. Circular radial meters provide the same telemetry in a compact, square footprint (12x12px), maximizing usable hierarchy width.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields or properties with [HierarchyRadialGauge]:

  • Ultra-Compact Donut Ring: Fits neatly on any hierarchy row without crowding text or other icons.
  • Normalized Range Mapping: Normalizes values between custom MinValue and MaxValue (e.g., 0f to 100f).
  • Smooth Arc Drawing: Uses procedural anti-aliased radial arc geometry for crisp rendering.
  • Play Mode Telemetry: Updates smoothly every frame as energy, stamina, or heat levels change during gameplay.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

{
    [HierarchyRadialGauge(MinValue = 0f, MaxValue = 100f, RingColor = "#38BDF8")]
    public float currentStamina = 75f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchySparkline]

[HierarchySparkline]

Renders a live rolling mini sparkline graph of historic numeric values directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchySparkline] records recent numeric values across consecutive editor frames and draws a live, rolling micro-sparkline graph on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchySparklineRenderer, it maintains a sliding window of historical points (HistoryLength = 24), normalizes points between MinValue and MaxValue, and plots a smoothed line graph (HierarchyDrawUtilities.DrawSparkline) within a sleek dark box (BackgroundColor = "#1E272E", LineColor = "#00FFAA").

The Problem: Diagnosing performance spikes, frame time hiccups, fluctuating network ping, or erratic physics velocities cannot be done with instantaneous numbers alone. A single number like 42ms doesn't reveal whether latency is holding steady or oscillating erratically. Opening Unity's Profiler or external graphing tools can be overkill for quick balance passes.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields or properties with [HierarchySparkline]:

  • Live Historical Trendline: Watch a continuous rolling plot of the last 24+ data points directly beside the GameObject.
  • Immediate Anomaly Detection: Easily spot erratic oscillations, periodic spikes, or sudden drops.
  • Configurable Normalization: Set fixed expected min/max boundaries or let values scale within range.
  • Compact Card Container: Embedded cleanly in the row (Width = 50f, Height = 12f) with rounded corners.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class PerformanceMonitor : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchySparkline(MinValue = 0f, MaxValue = 60f, LineColor = "#10B981")]
    public float frameTimeMs;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyStateSnapshot]

[HierarchyStateSnapshot]

Displays a real-time sliding history breadcrumb of finite state transitions directly on the hierarchy row during Play Mode.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStateSnapshot] captures finite state machine transitions and displays a real-time sliding breadcrumb trail (e.g. Patrol → Chase → Attack) on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyStateSnapshotRenderer, it detects whenever the monitored state variable changes, adds the new state to an internal sliding history buffer (MaxHistoryCount = 3), formats past states in subtle gray (HistoryColor = "#7F8C8D"), and highlights the active state in bright green (ActiveColor = "#2ECC71").

The Problem: When debugging complex AI behaviors or character state machines, unexpected bugs frequently arise from invalid transition sequences (e.g., an AI skipping from Stunned directly into Attack without going through Recover). Catching these momentary skips in real time by looking at a single instantaneous state variable in the Inspector is nearly impossible.

The Solution: Decorating state variables with [HierarchyStateSnapshot]:

  • Real-Time Transition Breadcrumbs: Immediately observe how an entity arrived at its current state (e.g. Idle → Alert → Chase).
  • Configurable History Depth: Retain the last 2, 3, or more consecutive states in the sliding window.
  • Color-Coded Status Distinction: Active state is clearly highlighted while historical precursors remain dim.
  • Detailed History Tooltips: Hovering over the badge displays full transition history descriptions.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public enum AIState { Idle, Patrol, Investigate, Combat }

public class SentryBot : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyStateSnapshot(MaxHistoryCount = 3, ActiveColor = "#10B981")]
    public AIState botState = AIState.Idle;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Telemetry & Visualizers/[HierarchyVectorDisplay]

[HierarchyVectorDisplay]

Formats Vector2 or Vector3 coordinates into a compact badge on the hierarchy row with optional magnitude info.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyVectorDisplay] formats 2D and 3D vector coordinates (Vector2, Vector3) into a sleek, compact badge on the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyVectorDisplayRenderer, it displays coordinates cleanly (e.g., (1.2, 0.0, -4.5)), with options to format numeric precision (Format = "{0:F1}"), customize colors (TextColor = "#B8E994", BadgeColor = "#1E272E"), and append the Euclidean length magnitude (ShowMagnitude = true, e.g. |4.7| (1.2, 0.0, -4.5)).

The Problem: Tracking spatial data (velocities, target offsets, aim directions, patrol waypoints, physics forces) requires opening the Inspector and expanding vector foldouts. When debugging multiple physics bodies or wandering AI units, comparing velocities or verifying whether a vector is zeroed is tedious.

The Solution: Decorating vector fields or properties with [HierarchyVectorDisplay]:

  • At-a-Glance Coordinate Telemetry: See current position offsets, velocities, or directional vectors directly in the hierarchy.
  • Euclidean Magnitude Readout: Instantly verify whether a velocity vector is nonzero or within threshold speed (ShowMagnitude = true).
  • Compact Badge Styling: Encapsulates numbers in a clean rounded dark pill badge.
  • Zero Inspector Distraction: Inspect multiple moving entities simultaneously while keeping Scene View unobstructed.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Tree & Structure/[HierarchyBookmark]

[HierarchyBookmark]

Marks a high-priority GameObject with a colored bookmark ribbon and quick slot indicator directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBookmark] anchors a prominent, colored bookmark ribbon (BookmarkColor = "#FF5E57") with a slot index (e.g. "#1", "#2") directly on the hierarchy row of high-priority GameObjects. Driven by HierarchyBookmarkRenderer, clicking the bookmark ribbon immediately highlights and selects the GameObject (Selection.activeGameObject = go) and pings it via EditorGUIUtility.PingObject(go).

The Problem: In large, multi-branch hierarchies with hundreds or thousands of entities, finding the key manager, the level exit trigger, the current player rig, or a specific debug spawner requires scrolling up and down through the list repeatedly or typing into the search filter (which collapses the tree hierarchy view).

The Solution: Decorating critical scene objects with [HierarchyBookmark]:

  • Visual Bookmark Ribbon: Instantly spot key landmark objects even when scrolling past dense trees.
  • Single-Click Selection & Ping: Click the ribbon to select and ping the object without needing to click its text label.
  • Slot Index or Custom Text: Display a slot number (#1, #2) or a short custom label (Label = "KEY").
  • Custom Color Ribbons: Assign different ribbon colors (e.g., cyan for lighting, red for boss rigs, green for spawners).

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Tree & Structure/[HierarchyBreadcrumb]

[HierarchyBreadcrumb]

Displays a compact ancestor hierarchy path breadcrumb next to the GameObject name.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBreadcrumb] traces the GameObject's parent hierarchy tree upwards and displays a compact ancestor breadcrumb trail (e.g. Environment › Sector_A › Lights ›) directly on the right side of the hierarchy row. Driven by HierarchyBreadcrumbRenderer, it formats ancestor names with a custom delimiter (Delimiter = " › "), limits traversal up to MaxDepth (default 3), and renders the path in subtle slate typography (TextColor = "#718093").

The Problem: In deeply nested scene trees (prefabs within prefabs, complex bone armatures, UI canvas hierarchies), finding out where an entity lives within the parent structure requires scrolling up to look for parent folder nodes or checking the Inspector's breadcrumb bar. When multiple objects share identical names (e.g., several objects named Light, Collider, or Anchor), confusion is common.

The Solution: Decorating nested components with [HierarchyBreadcrumb]:

  • Contextual Parent Awareness: See parent and grandparent container names at a glance without collapsing branches.
  • Disambiguate Identical Names: Immediately tell apart identically named objects based on their parent path.
  • Configurable Ancestor Depth: Cap depth (MaxDepth = 3) to keep breadcrumbs concise and uncluttered.
  • Custom Delimiters & Colors: Choose symbols like " › ", " / ", or " → " with custom text colors.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyBreadcrumb(MaxDepth = 2, Delimiter = " / ")]
public class PropDetailFixture : MonoBehaviour
{
    public int lodLevel = 0;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Tree & Structure/[HierarchyChildCounter]

[HierarchyChildCounter]

Displays a badge of active and total direct or recursive child GameObjects directly on the hierarchy row.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyChildCounter] inspects a GameObject's transform hierarchy and renders a badge showing active versus total child counts (e.g. "3/5", "12/20"). Driven by HierarchyChildCounterRenderer, it supports direct child counting (Recursive = false) or recursive descendant counting across all sub-branches (Recursive = true). It allows hiding the badge when empty (OnlyIfChildrenExist = true), customizable string templates (Format = "{active}/{total}"), and empty warning badge alerts (WarnIfEmpty = true, EmptyWarningColor = "#E71C23").

The Problem: When organizing object pools, waypoint networks, patrol paths, spawn groups, or UI lists, collapsed parent objects hide how many children are inside and whether any are currently active. Developers must repeatedly expand and collapse foldouts just to check child counts.

The Solution: Decorating parent container components with [HierarchyChildCounter]:

  • Active / Total Child Telemetry: See exactly how many children exist and how many are currently active (e.g., spawned vs unspawned).
  • Direct vs Recursive Modes: Count immediate children or full subtree descendants across all nested depths.
  • Empty Group Alerts: Flashes a red warning badge when an object expected to have children is empty (WarnIfEmpty = true).
  • Custom Badge Formatting: Customize background colors, text colors, and format strings (e.g. "{active} Active").

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class WaypointGroup : MonoBehaviour
{
    public bool isLooping = true;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Tree & Structure/[HierarchyFolder]

[HierarchyFolder]

Transforms an empty GameObject into an organizational folder header with folder styling and collapse indicators.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyFolder] converts empty organizational GameObjects into clean, stylized virtual folders directly within the Unity Hierarchy window. Driven by HierarchyFolderRenderer, it paints a full-width background header row (HeaderColor = "#1E272E"), an accent stripe along the left edge (FolderColor = "#F39C12"), a bold folder icon ("📁 "), and customized title text. It supports an optional build-time optimization (FlattenOnBuild = true) to unparent and flatten child objects during player builds, preventing unnecessary transform hierarchy overhead in shipping games.

The Problem: Unity does not provide native folders for the Hierarchy window. Developers create empty dummy GameObjects as pseudo-folders (e.g. [CAMERAS], _LIGHTS_, --- ENVIRONMENT ---). These dummy objects look messy, lack folder iconography, and introduce deep transform hierarchy matrices at runtime that can negatively affect performance if left unmanaged.

The Solution: Decorating organizational GameObjects with [HierarchyFolder]:

  • Native-Feeling Folder Headers: Displays full-width colored folder rows with distinct folder icons (📁).
  • Clean Visual Chapters: Visually divides scenes into logical chapters (e.g., Audio, Lighting, Spawners, UI).
  • Optional Flatten-On-Build: Unparents children during standalone build compilation to optimize runtime transform depth.
  • Custom Folder & Header Colors: Assign distinct theme colors to different folder types (amber for lights, blue for UI, purple for VFX).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyFolder(FolderName = "STATIC ENVIRONMENT", FolderColor = "#10B981")]
public class EnvironmentFolder : MonoBehaviour
{
}

Hierarchy Designer/Tree & Structure/[HierarchyTreeBranch]

[HierarchyTreeBranch]

Draws visual tree branch connector lines linking parents to children in the hierarchy window.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTreeBranch] renders smooth, anti-aliased guide lines (using Handles.DrawAAPolyLine) and endpoint junction dots (Handles.DrawSolidDisc) connecting nested child GameObjects directly to their parent foldout anchor in the Unity Hierarchy window.

The Problem: In standard Unity, child indentations in the Hierarchy window are subtle and lack vertical guide lines. In deep hierarchies (such as skeletal rigs, complex UI canvases, or nested environmental prefabs), it is difficult to visually trace which parent a deeply nested entity belongs to, often leading to accidental misparenting or dragging into wrong groups.

The Solution: Decorating parent or child MonoBehaviour classes with [HierarchyTreeBranch]:

  • Visual Branch Tracing: Renders clear L-shaped and T-shaped connector lines from the parent foldout level to each child row.
  • Terminal Dot Indicators: Optionally places crisp circular dots (ShowDots = true, DotRadius = 2.0f) at branch endpoints.
  • Custom Aesthetic Styling: Fully configurable line color (LineColor), line thickness (LineWidth), and opacity (LineAlpha).
  • Dynamic Sibling Handling: Automatically differentiates intermediate siblings (continuous vertical line) from the last sibling (terminating L-junction).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyTreeBranch(LineColor = "#5A8DEE", LineWidth = 1.5f, LineAlpha = 0.6f)]
public class NestedNodeComponent : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string nodeIdentifier;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyAnimatedText]

[HierarchyAnimatedText]

Animates the GameObject hierarchy label with real-time typography effects such as wavy motion, rainbow hue shifts, glitching, typewriter reveal, pulse, or neon flicker.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyAnimatedText] applies procedural real-time typography animations to the GameObject's label directly within the Unity Hierarchy window. Supporting six distinct animation styles (Wavy, Rainbow, Glitch, Typewriter, Pulse, NeonFlicker), it transforms static hierarchy rows into lively visual landmarks.

The Problem: In expansive projects with hundreds of actors, spawners, and active subsystems, critical GameObjects (such as boss entities during active combat, streaming managers, or network beacons) easily get lost in the sea of monotone hierarchy labels. Standard Unity provides no built-in mechanism to highlight active or important objects dynamically.

The Solution: Applying [HierarchyAnimatedText]:

  • Six Procedural Animation Styles: Switch seamlessly between smooth sine-wave undulations (Wavy), chromatic cycling (Rainbow), digital distortion (Glitch), character-by-character reveal (Typewriter), rhythmic breathing (Pulse), and electric shimmer (NeonFlicker).
  • Granular Motion Parameters: Adjust oscillation amplitude, wave frequency, playback speed, and per-character phase delays.
  • Full Typography Control: Specify custom base colors (BaseColor = "#00FFAA"), bold/italic styling, and pixel font sizing.
  • Continuous Repaint Engine: Driven by an optimized hierarchy repaint lifecycle (Continuous = true) for buttery-smooth 60fps editor animations without runtime overhead.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyAnimatedText(
    AnimationMode = HierarchyTextAnimMode.Wavy, 
    Speed = 3.0f, 
    Amplitude = 3.0f, 
    BaseColor = "#00FFAA", 
    Bold = true)]
public class BossEncounterManager : MonoBehaviour
{
    public bool isEncounterActive = true;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyBouncingText]

[HierarchyBouncingText]

Animates each character of the hierarchy label in a staggered vertical bouncing spring sequence.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBouncingText] calculates a sinusoidal or elastic vertical bounce displacement for each character of the GameObject's hierarchy label, applying a time-delay phase stagger between consecutive glyphs to produce a lively kinetic wave.

The Problem: In playful, casual, or stylized arcade projects, standard static text labels in the Hierarchy window can feel bland. Furthermore, highlighting key interactive elements (such as bouncy platforms, power-up pickups, or celebratory loot chests) is difficult using flat colors alone.

The Solution: Decorating game classes with [HierarchyBouncingText]:

  • Dynamic Character-by-Character Stagger: Consecutive characters bounce with an adjustable offset delay (Stagger = 0.2f), creating an organic accordion wave.
  • Physical Spring Feel: Configurable bounce height (BounceHeight = 3.5f) and cycle frequency (Speed = 3.0f) bring energetic life to key gameplay entities.
  • Vibrant Font Styling: Supports custom hex color tinting (TextColor = "#F1C40F") and bold font weight.
  • Editor-Friendly Repainting: Smoothly repaints without hitching editor responsiveness or generating garbage allocation.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class CoinPickup : MonoBehaviour
{
    public int coinValue = 10;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

    TextColor = "#10B981", 
    BounceHeight = 4.5f,

public class SpringBouncerPlatform : MonoBehaviour

    public float launchImpulse = 15.0f;
    public AudioClip bounceSfx;
    public ParticleSystem impactDust;

    private void OnTriggerEnter(Collider other)
    {
        if (other.attachedRigidbody != null)
        {
            other.attachedRigidbody.linearVelocity = Vector3.up * launchImpulse;
        }
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyBreathingColorText]

[HierarchyBreathingColorText]

Smoothly interpolates hierarchy label text color between two hues in a continuous breathing sine wave.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyBreathingColorText] continuously blends the GameObject's label text between a PrimaryColor and a SecondaryColor using smooth sinusoidal interpolation (Mathf.Sin(EditorApplication.timeSinceStartup * Speed)).

The Problem: Static color labels in the hierarchy can become visually monotonous, while aggressive kinetic animations (such as shaking, bouncing, or glitching) can be distracting for systems that simply require ambient, calm emphasis.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyBreathingColorText]:

  • Ambient Chromatic Pulsing: Gently shifts the text hue back and forth between two curated colors (e.g., cyan to magenta, or amber to deep orange) without distracting character displacement.
  • Adjustable Breathing Cadence: Control how fast the color transition oscillates (Speed = 1.5f).
  • Bold Weight Support: Enhances color legibility across both dark and light editor themes.
  • Zero Physics & Clean Rendering: Uses standard Unity GUI label drawing with zero scene overhead.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyClippedOverflowText]

[HierarchyClippedOverflowText]

Truncates long hierarchy labels with an ellipsis to prevent clutter, with optional marquee scrolling on hover.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyClippedOverflowText] caps excessively long GameObject names in the Hierarchy window at a specified character limit (MaxCharacters = 18), appending an ellipsis (...). When the user moves the mouse cursor over the row, an optional horizontal marquee animation smoothly scrolls the complete name so no information is lost.

The Problem: In production pipelines (such as imported FBX rigs, photogrammetry scans, procedural dungeon rooms, or serialized network entity instances), GameObjects often have verbose names like ModularMedievalCastle_WallSegment_VariationB_LOD0_CollisionAnchor. These long labels push right-side controls off screen and create visual clutter in the Hierarchy window.

The Solution: Decorating verbose prefabs or components with [HierarchyClippedOverflowText]:

  • Clean Truncation: Clips the visible label neatly to a uniform width (MaxCharacters = 18).
  • Interactive Hover Marquee: Hovering over the item triggers an automatic ticker tape scroll (MarqueeOnHover = true, ScrollSpeed = 3.0f) to reveal the full original identifier.
  • Custom Font Color: Colorizes the visible name cleanly (TextColor = "#E0E0E0").
  • Protects Layout Real Estate: Preserves right-side margin space for icons, toggles, and metadata badges.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyClippedOverflowText(MaxCharacters = 16, MarqueeOnHover = true, ScrollSpeed = 2.5f)]
public class EnvironmentalDungeonProp : MonoBehaviour
{
    public int lodLevel = 0;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyDecryptText]

[HierarchyDecryptText]

Scrambles hierarchy label characters through cyber glyphs before resolving into the real GameObject name.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyDecryptText] executes a sci-fi matrix cypher decryption sequence across the GameObject hierarchy label. At regular intervals (Interval = 4.0f), characters scramble through randomized alphanumeric symbols in DecryptColor before locking one-by-one into their resolved name in TextColor.

The Problem: In futuristic, cyberpunk, or hacking-themed projects, standard hierarchy trees lack immersive flavor. Developers and designers working on AI directors, terminal consoles, security grids, or encryption puzzles often want these systems to visually stand out in the hierarchy with a distinctive hacker aesthetic.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyDecryptText]:

  • Procedural Cypher Scrambling: Replaces characters with rapidly alternating matrix glyphs that resolve left-to-right over a configured duration (GlitchDuration = 1.2f).
  • Dual Hue Design: Configures distinct colors for unresolved scrambling glyphs (DecryptColor = "#00FFAA") versus locked characters (TextColor = "#2ECC71").
  • Periodic Cycling: Automatically repeats the decryption cycle at user-defined intervals (Interval = 4.0f).
  • Bold Styling: Ensures glyphs remain sharp and legible across all Editor scale factors.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyGradientText]

[HierarchyGradientText]

Renders a character-by-character color gradient or dynamic rainbow effect across the hierarchy label.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyGradientText] interpolates colors across each individual glyph in the GameObject name, supporting 2-stop linear gradients (StartColor → EndColor), 3-stop multi-color ramps (MiddleColor), and dynamic cycling chromatic spectrums (RainbowMode = true).

The Problem: Standard Unity text color customization is strictly uniform and monolithic across the entire name. In complex project architectures, differentiating high-level tiers (e.g. Legendary items, Epic boss rigs, or special engine subsystems) with single solid colors lacks visual prestige.

The Solution: Decorating classes with [HierarchyGradientText]:

  • Smooth Character Gradient Interpolation: Calculates precise per-character t-values from start to finish, rendering a luxury gradient banner across the text.
  • 3-Stop Color Ramps: Supports intermediate midpoint hues (MiddleColor = "#E67E22") for fire, sunset, or galaxy color blends.
  • Dynamic Rainbow Spectrum Mode: Toggle RainbowMode = true to animate the entire visible character sequence through the full HSV spectrum continuously.
  • Weight and Style Options: Fully supports bold weight, italic slant, and custom pixel font sizes.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

  • Crisp Custom Tinting: Applies custom hex color palette styling (TextColor = "#E0E0E0").

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyNumberedSequence]

[HierarchyNumberedSequence]

Formats sibling GameObject hierarchy names with automatic sequential indices or Roman numerals.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyNumberedSequence] automatically prepends or formats the GameObject name with its sibling order index, supporting standard numbers (1, 2, 3), zero-padded indices (01, 02, 03), Roman numerals (I, II, III), or hexadecimal notation (0x01, 0x02), using a flexible format template like "[{num}] {name}".

The Problem: When designing ordered sequences (such as checkpoint sequences, patrol waypoints, dialogue beat triggers, or inventory slots), developers often have to manually rename GameObjects to maintain order (e.g. Waypoint_01, Waypoint_02). When re-ordering or inserting a new waypoint, all subsequent items must be laboriously renumbered by hand.

The Solution: Decorating sequential components with [HierarchyNumberedSequence]:

  • Dynamic Sibling Indexing: Queries transform.GetSiblingIndex() + 1 dynamically at repaint time, meaning reordering objects in the hierarchy immediately updates their displayed sequence number automatically.
  • Multiple Numbering Styles: Choose from Standard, ZeroPadded, RomanNumeral, or Hex formats.
  • Custom Display Templates: Use placeholders {num}, {roman}, and {name} in custom format strings (e.g. "STAGE {roman} : {name}").
  • Two-Tone Color Highlighting: Distinguish the sequence number (NumberColor = "#F39C12") from the object name (TextColor = "#E0E0E0").

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyNumberedSequence(Format = HierarchyNumberFormat.ZeroPadded, Template = "[{num}] {name}")]
public class PatrolWaypoint : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float waitDuration = 2.0f;
}

[HierarchyStateColorText]

Dynamically tints the hierarchy label based on whether a serialized numeric field satisfies low, mid, or high thresholds.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStateColorText] links the color of the GameObject's hierarchy row label directly to a serialized numeric field (such as health, fuel, temperature, or aiThreatLevel). Driven by HierarchyStateColorTextRenderer, it compares the live runtime or serialized value against HighThreshold and LowThreshold, dynamically coloring the name green (HighColor), yellow (MidColor), or red (LowColor).

The Problem: During playtesting and scene debugging, tracking which AI actors are wounded, which generators are out of fuel, or which physics bodies have exceeded critical velocity thresholds requires clicking on each object individually to check the Inspector.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [HierarchyStateColorText]:

  • At-a-Glance Status Monitoring: Immediately spot low-health enemies, depleted resources, or overheated systems directly from the Hierarchy window without selecting them.
  • Three-Tier Threshold Grading: Configure custom boundaries (HighThreshold = 70f, LowThreshold = 30f) to seamlessly map healthy, warning, and critical states.
  • Custom Color Triad: Set tailored hex colors for high (HighColor), medium (MidColor), and low (LowColor) states.
  • Zero Polling Lag: Evaluates the member via reflection during IMGUI repaint passes, reflecting live changes immediately during Play Mode.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class EnemyHealth : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HierarchyStateColorText(HighThreshold = 75f, LowThreshold = 25f)]
    public float currentHealth = 100f;
}

csharp

        HighThreshold = 80f,

        MidColor = "#F59E0B", 
        LowColor = "#EF4444", 
        Bold = true)]
    public float coreIntegrity = 100.0f;

    [Header("Cooling System")]
    public float coolantFlowRate = 12.5f;
    public bool emergencyVentsOpen = false;

    public void ApplyDamage(float amount)
    {
        coreIntegrity = Mathf.Clamp(coreIntegrity - amount, 0f, 100f);
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyStylishText]

[HierarchyStylishText]

Applies text casing, decorative framing brackets, font styling, and optional background pill boxes to hierarchy labels.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyStylishText] provides an all-in-one typography and framing suite for GameObject hierarchy labels. It combines automated text casing (Uppercase, SmallCaps, TitleCase, etc.), decorative unicode framing glyphs ([ ], { }, » «, ✦ ✦, ◈ ◈, //), custom font sizing, bold/italic weights, and optional background pill boxes with rounded corners (BorderRadius).

The Problem: Creating distinct visual categories in large scene hierarchies usually requires manually typing special symbols into GameObject names (e.g. [MANAGERS], --- ENVIRONMENT ---, >> UI <<). This pollutes runtime object names, creates inconsistencies across team members, and clutters asset references.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyStylishText]:

  • Six Casing Modes: Convert names automatically to Uppercase, Lowercase, Spaced, SmallCaps, or TitleCase without modifying the actual gameObject.name.
  • Decorative Framing Glyphs: Surround names with framing brackets (Brackets, Curly, Arrows, Stars, Cyber, or Slash).
  • Background Pill Box: Draw an optional rounded container box (BackgroundColor = "#1E293B", BorderRadius = 4f) behind the label.
  • Custom Prefix & Suffix: Prepend and append custom string tags directly in the editor display.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

    Casing = HierarchyTextCasing.Uppercase, 
    Frame = HierarchyTextFrame.Cyber, 
    TextColor = "#38BDF8", 
    Bold = true)]
public class CoreSystemManager : MonoBehaviour
{
    public bool isInitialized = true;
}

[HierarchySubtitleText]

Renders a secondary subtext or live field value subtitle next to the GameObject name in the hierarchy.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchySubtitleText] renders a secondary sub-label directly beside or beneath the GameObject name in the Unity Hierarchy window. It supports either static secondary text (StaticSubtitle = "LOD 0") or dynamic live reflection binding to a component field (FieldName = "currentScore" or FieldName = "currentState"), formatted through a customizable string template (Format = "↳ {0}").

The Problem: Developers frequently need quick visibility into secondary properties—such as the active state machine state, current AI target, pool capacity, or prefab variation—without constantly selecting objects and digging through Inspector tabs.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchySubtitleText]:

  • Live Reflection Binding: Automatically queries the specified field (FieldName) on the component and formats its value in real time.
  • Static Subtitle Fallback: Allows quick static descriptive tags (StaticSubtitle = "Main Canvas") when dynamic reflection is not required.
  • Customizable Format Templates: Supports custom template prefixes and symbols (Format = "↳ {0}", "[{0}]", ":: {0}").
  • Muted Subtext Styling: Rendered in a refined muted tone (TextColor = "#718093") with italic styling so it complements rather than overshadows the main object name.

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyTextColor]

[HierarchyTextColor]

Customizes hierarchy label text color, font style, size, affixes, and optional background pill box.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTextColor] is the primary workhorse attribute for colorizing and formatting GameObject names in the Unity Hierarchy window. It provides direct hex color tinting (ColorHex = "#FFD700"), font weight controls (Bold, Italic, FontSize), optional text affixes (Prefix, Suffix), and an optional background pill box with customizable padding (BackgroundColor, BackgroundPadding).

The Problem: By default, Unity renders all hierarchy labels in a single monochromatic gray/white font. In complex scenes, finding essential landmark objects—such as main cameras, player rigs, game managers, or level exit triggers—requires reading through hundreds of identical rows.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyTextColor]:

  • Instant Color Identification: Tint critical objects with distinct color codes (e.g. gold for managers, cyan for lighting, emerald for checkpoints).
  • Flexible Overload Constructors: Accepts hex color strings directly in the constructor ([HierarchyTextColor("#38BDF8")]) or parameterless for default gold ("#FFD700").
  • Typography Sizing & Weights: Fine-tune font sizes and weights for visual hierarchy.
  • Background Pill Box: Draw an optional colored pill behind the text for prominent header badges.

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyTextShadow]

[HierarchyTextShadow]

Renders a directional drop shadow or omnidirectional glowing halo behind hierarchy label characters.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTextShadow] adds depth and contrast to the GameObject's hierarchy row label. It draws a directional drop shadow (ShadowOffsetX, ShadowOffsetY) or an 8-way omnidirectional glowing halo (GlowHalo = true) behind the foreground characters, significantly improving text readability against alternating hierarchy background strips.

The Problem: In custom Editor themes, selected rows, or when multiple background colors and zebra-striping plugins are in use, hierarchy text can lose contrast and become difficult to read. Furthermore, flat text lacks visual punch.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyTextShadow]:

  • Directional Drop Shadows: Offsets a crisp shadow silhouette (ShadowOffsetX = 1.0f, ShadowOffsetY = 1.0f) beneath the label for elevated contrast.
  • 8-Way Halo Glow: Enabling GlowHalo = true renders an omnidirectional outline around each glyph, creating a neon or radiant outline effect.
  • Independent Color Control: Configure foreground text (TextColor = "#FFFFFF") and shadow/glow tint (ShadowColor = "#000000") separately.
  • Typography Weights: Full support for bold and italic font variations.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyTooltip]

[HierarchyTooltip]

Displays a rich hover tooltip over the GameObject row, showing optional notes, category, author, status, and components.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTooltip] displays a comprehensive metadata tooltip window when hovering over the GameObject's row in the Unity Hierarchy window. Driven by HierarchyTooltipRenderer, it composes multi-line documentation including category tags (Category), developer notes (Tooltip), author attribution (Author), active/inactive status (ShowActiveState), and an automatic attached component summary (ShowComponentSummary).

The Problem: In team environments with multiple level designers, technical artists, and programmers, inspecting unfamiliar GameObjects requires selecting them, scrolling through the Inspector, and deciphering which components are attached and what purpose the object serves. Standard Unity provides no tooltip mechanism on hierarchy rows.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyTooltip]:

  • Instant On-Hover Documentation: Hovering reveals developer notes without selecting or deselecting the active scene context.
  • Component Stack Inspection: Enabling ShowComponentSummary = true dynamically discovers and lists all attached non-Transform components (Components (4): MeshFilter, MeshRenderer, BoxCollider, AudioSource).
  • Team Attribution & Categorization: Displays who created or owns the subsystem (Author = "Soutick") and which department it belongs to (Category = "Gameplay / AI").
  • Flexible Overload Constructors: Set simple tooltip strings directly ([HierarchyTooltip("Main cinematic camera rig")]) or configure advanced metadata properties.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

public class LevelStreamingController : MonoBehaviour
{
    public string targetSceneName = "Zone_02";
}

Hierarchy Designer/Typography & Naming/[HierarchyUnderlineText]

[HierarchyUnderlineText]

Draws a styled colored underline, dotted line, dashed rule, or strikethrough stroke beneath or across the hierarchy label.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyUnderlineText] draws customizable geometric stroke lines beneath or through the GameObject label in the Unity Hierarchy window. Supporting solid lines, dotted patterns, dashed strokes, and strikethrough styling (LineStyle), it enables technical artists and scene designers to emphasize critical scene anchors, mark deprecated or disabled entities with strikethroughs, or categorize systems with color-coded underlines.

The Problem: In expansive scenes containing hundreds of GameObjects, standard Unity hierarchy rows offer uniform styling with no typographic weight or visual hierarchy. Identifying marked milestones, deprecation states, or primary game subsystem roots requires manual folder inspection or custom selection tags.

The Solution: Decorating components or fields with [HierarchyUnderlineText]:

  • Visual Emphasis & Hierarchy: Underline primary subsystem roots (e.g. [GameDirector], [PlayerSpawner]) with sharp, vivid stroke accents.
  • Deprecation & Strikethrough States: Mark outdated prefabs or legacy systems scheduled for decommissioning (LineStyle = HierarchyLineStyle.Strikethrough) without deleting them from source control.
  • Multiple Stroke Styles: Choose between Solid, Dotted, Dashed, and Strikethrough rules with pixel-perfect stroke thickness (LineThickness).
  • Typographic Customization: Independently style label color (TextColor), stroke color (LineColor), and font styles (Bold, Italic).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

    TextColor = "#FFFFFF",
    LineColor = "#FF5E57",

PropertyTypeDefaultDescription

HierarchyLineStyle Enum Values

  • Solid: Continuous single line beneath the text baseline.
  • Dotted: Equidistant square dot sequence along the baseline.
  • Dashed: Segmented dashes across the length of the label.
  • Strikethrough: Centered horizontal line drawn across the midpoint of the text glyphs.

csharp
using UnityEngine;

        TextColor = "#FFF200",

        LineStyle = HierarchyLineStyle.Solid,
        Bold = true)]

        public bool isCheckpointActive = true;
    }

[HierarchyTransformWarning]ClassDisplays an alert badge if the GameObject has non-uniform or negative scale values.
Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyLayerCollisionMatrix]

[HierarchyLayerCollisionMatrix]

Displays a compact hierarchy badge showing how many layers can physically collide with this GameObject's layer.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyLayerCollisionMatrix] renders an interactive telemetry badge directly on the GameObject's hierarchy row indicating how many physics layers are configured to collide with the GameObject's current layer in Unity's Physics Collision Matrix. It supports both 3D Physics (Physics.GetIgnoreLayerCollision) and 2D Physics (Physics2D.GetIgnoreLayerCollision).

The Problem: Diagnosing unintended physics interactions or dead collision events in Unity requires opening Project Settings > Physics or Physics 2D, scrolling through a massive grid of 32x32 checkboxes, and manually calculating which layer combinations are active. When objects are assigned improper layers (e.g. Default colliding with everything, or a Projectile layer colliding with nothing), physics performance degrades or critical gameplay triggers fail silently.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyLayerCollisionMatrix]:

  • Instant Collision Audit: See at a glance how many layers collide with this object (e.g. ⚡ 6 Layers or ⚡ 0 Layers if completely ignored).
  • Zero Configuration Setup: Automatically extracts the GameObject's active layer (gameObject.layer) and cross-references the project collision matrix.
  • 2D & 3D Physics Modes: Toggle between 3D physics evaluation and 2D physics matrix with a single boolean property (Show2DPhysics = true).
  • Visual Styling: Customize badge background (BadgeColor), text color (TextColor), and border outline (BorderColor).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyLayerCollisionMatrix]
public class CombatHitbox : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float damage = 25f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyMissingScriptDetector]

[HierarchyMissingScriptDetector]

Highlights GameObjects containing broken or missing MonoBehaviour scripts with an attention-grabbing hazard warning badge.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyMissingScriptDetector] proactively monitors attached components on the GameObject and flags missing or corrupted MonoBehaviour scripts (Component == null references caused by deleted, renamed, or uncompiled C# scripts) with an immediate, high-visibility hazard warning badge in the Hierarchy window. It optionally renders animated or static diagonal hazard caution stripes (ShowHazardStripe = true).

The Problem: Missing scripts ("The associated script can not be loaded. Please fix any compile errors...") are notorious silent bugs in Unity. Unity provides no native warning indicator in the Hierarchy view; developers must expand every GameObject and scroll through each Inspector component stack to locate missing script placeholders. Uncaught missing scripts often trigger runtime NullReferenceExceptions or break automated build pipelines.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyMissingScriptDetector]:

  • Instant Hierarchy Warning: Spot broken scripts immediately in the scene tree without selecting each object.
  • Hazard Caution Stripes: Draws high-contrast diagonal safety stripes (ShowHazardStripe) across the alert badge to catch attention.
  • Customizable Warning Colors: Customize alert tint (AlertColor) to fit studio QA guidelines.
  • Continuous Scene Health: Automatically updates as scripts are re-imported, fixed, or removed.

2. Quick Start

csharp

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyNamingConvention]

[HierarchyNamingConvention]

Enforces scene naming conventions (mandatory affixes, disallowed whitespace) and displays an alert icon on rule violations.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyNamingConvention] enforces team and studio naming standards on GameObjects decorated with marked components. It validates that the host GameObject begins with a mandatory prefix (Prefix), concludes with a mandatory suffix (Suffix), and adheres to whitespace rules (AllowSpaces = false). If the GameObject name violates any rule, an eye-catching warning badge is rendered directly on the hierarchy row.

The Problem: In multi-developer studios, inconsistent naming (e.g. Light_Spot, SpotLight, spot light (1)) breaks automated search tools, scene parsing scripts, and CI build validators. Enforcing conventions through manual code reviews or external linters creates friction and interrupts level designer workflows.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyNamingConvention]:

  • Real-Time Visual Feedback: Immediate visual warning badges appear in the Hierarchy window when an artist or designer creates or renames an object improperly.
  • Prefix & Suffix Validation: Enforce prefix constraints (e.g. Prefix = "SYS_" or Prefix = "UI_") and suffix standards (e.g. Suffix = "_Trigger").
  • Whitespace Sanitation: Set AllowSpaces = false to forbid spaces in GameObject names, preventing broken paths in string-based resource loaders or asset bundles.
  • Custom Alert Color: Adjust the warning pill tint (WarnColor) to match warning levels across project categories.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyNamingConvention(
    Prefix = "SPAWN_",
    Suffix = "_Point",
    AllowSpaces = false)]
public class SpawnPointMarker : MonoBehaviour
{
    public int teamId = 1;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyPrefabState]

[HierarchyPrefabState]

Displays hierarchy indicators for Prefab variant depth, overridden property counts, and broken or disconnected instances.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyPrefabState] renders informative prefab status telemetry directly within the Unity Hierarchy window. It visually indicates whether a GameObject is a connected prefab asset, a prefab variant, an instance with local overrides (displaying the exact number of modified properties via ShowOverrideCount = true), or a corrupted/disconnected instance with a missing asset reference.

The Problem: In large scenes built from modular prefab kits, accidental overrides (such as position shifts, altered material assignments, or modified component values) go unnoticed until team members attempt to push updates back to the source prefab. Unity's standard hierarchy displays a faint blue icon for overrides, but provides no visibility into how many properties have diverged or whether nested child objects have disconnected.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyPrefabState]:

  • Real-Time Override Count: Displays the exact tally of modified serialized properties directly on the hierarchy row (e.g. ⛯ +3 Overrides).
  • Broken Reference Alerts: Instantly highlights disconnected or broken prefab instances in striking hazard red (BrokenColor), preventing lost assets before builds.
  • Custom Status Color Palette: Separate distinct colors for healthy overrides (OverrideColor) and critical broken instances (BrokenColor).
  • Clean Level Assembly: Keeps level design teams aware of accidental prefab modifications before staging Git commits.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyPrefabState]
public class ModularWallSegment : MonoBehaviour
{
    public Material wallMaterial;
    public bool hasDoorOpening = false;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyRaycastAudit]

[HierarchyRaycastAudit]

Audits UI graphics for wasted raycast calculations and provides an instant one-click optimization fix directly in the hierarchy.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyRaycastAudit] inspects Unity UI components (Graphic, Image, Text, TextMeshProUGUI) on the GameObject. If non-interactive graphics have raycastTarget = true enabled (such as background plates, static icons, or decorative borders that never receive clicks), it displays an alert badge in the Hierarchy window. When EnableOneClickFix = true, clicking the badge in the Hierarchy immediately sets raycastTarget = false and marks the scene dirty.

The Problem: In standard Unity UI (uGUI), newly created Image and Text components enable Raycast Target by default. As UI scenes expand, hundreds of non-interactive elements are tested every frame during EventSystem raycasting. This produces substantial CPU overhead, canvas rebuild penalties, and input latency—especially on mobile and VR devices. Manually hunting down and unchecking every redundant raycast target across dozens of nested prefabs is tedious and error-prone.

The Solution: Decorating UI components with [HierarchyRaycastAudit]:

  • Automated Raycast Auditing: Instantly identifies decorative UI elements wasting raycasts (ShowOnlyIfWastingRaycasts = true).
  • One-Click Instant Fix: Simply click the hierarchy warning badge to automatically disable raycastTarget on all redundant graphics on that object.
  • Safe State Indicators: When ShowOnlyIfWastingRaycasts = false, renders an unobtrusive green badge (SafeColor) confirming the object is optimized.
  • Measurable Mobile Performance: Dramatically slashes CPU time inside GraphicRaycaster.Raycast during heavy touch/click interactions.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyRaycastAudit]
public class InventorySlotUI : MonoBehaviour
{
    public int slotIndex;
}

public HierarchyRaycastAuditAttribute()
  • Parameterless constructor with one-click repair enabled and alert filtering focused strictly on wasted raycasts.

5. Properties & Customization Options

PropertyTypeDefaultDescription
ShowOnlyIfWastingRaycastsbooltrueWhen true, only displays the badge when decorative non-interactive graphics have raycasts enabled.
WarningColorstring"#C0392B"Hex color string for the warning badge when redundant raycast targets are detected.
SafeColorstring"#27AE60"Hex color string for the badge when all attached graphics are properly optimized.
EnableOneClickFixbooltrueWhen true, clicking the hierarchy badge immediately disables raycastTarget on redundant elements.

6. Real-World Production Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using UnityEngine.UI;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

namespace ProjectZero.UI
{
    // Health bar HUD with decorative borders and text audited for wasted raycasts
    [HierarchyRaycastAudit(
        ShowOnlyIfWastingRaycasts = true,
        WarningColor = "#E74C3C",
        EnableOneClickFix = true,
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public class PlayerHealthBarHUD : MonoBehaviour
    {
        [Header("UI References")]
        public Image healthFillImage;       // Fill bar - should not be a raycast target
        public Image frameBorderImage;      // Decorative frame - should not be a raycast target
        public Button useHealthPotionButton;// Interactive button - raycast target SHOULD remain true

        public void UpdateHealth(float current, float max)
        {
            if (healthFillImage != null)
                healthFillImage.fillAmount = current / max;
        }
    }
}

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyRequirementCheck]

[HierarchyRequirementCheck]

Validates required peer components on the same GameObject and displays an immediate alert badge if any are missing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyRequirementCheck] audits the GameObject's component stack against a specified list of required component types (params Type[] requiredTypes). If any required peer component is absent (e.g. missing Rigidbody, AudioSource, or Collider), it displays an unmistakable warning alert badge in the Hierarchy window displaying which components are missing.

The Problem: While Unity provides the native [RequireComponent] attribute, it only adds missing components at the exact moment the script is first attached. If a developer or artist subsequently removes the dependent component, or creates a prefab variant without it, Unity offers no hierarchy-level warning. Missing dependencies then trigger runtime NullReferenceExceptions when scripts attempt to call GetComponent<T>().

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyRequirementCheck]:

  • Continuous Hierarchy Validation: Continuously checks for the presence of required component types in real-time.
  • Support for Multiple Dependencies: Accepts any number of required component types via params Type[] (e.g. typeof(Rigidbody), typeof(Collider)).
  • Non-Intrusive Workflow: Unlike [RequireComponent], it does not forcefully instantiate components that might need specialized custom setup, allowing artists to configure dependencies deliberately.
  • Custom Alert Color: Configurable alert badge color (AlertColor) for rapid identification.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyRequirementCheck(typeof(Rigidbody), typeof(Collider))]
public class PhysicsDamager : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float impactForce = 100f;
}

Hierarchy Designer/Validation & Safety/[HierarchyTransformWarning]

[HierarchyTransformWarning]

Displays an alert badge if the GameObject has non-uniform or negative scale values, preventing physics and rendering bugs.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HierarchyTransformWarning] inspects the GameObject's local transform scale vector (transform.localScale). If the scale components are non-uniform (X != Y != Z, when CheckNonUniformScale = true) or if any component has negative values (X < 0 || Y < 0 || Z < 0, when CheckNegativeScale = true), it renders an immediate alert badge on the hierarchy row.

The Problem: In Unity 3D, non-uniform scaling on parent objects causes skewed child rotations, distorted capsule colliders, stretched particle emitters, and broken lighting normals. Negative scaling (often used by artists to flip meshes) can invert face normals, corrupt backface culling, and break PhysX collision detection algorithms. Discovering these transform anomalies usually only happens after bizarre physics or visual glitches occur in Play Mode.

The Solution: Decorating components with [HierarchyTransformWarning]:

  • Proactive Scale Guarding: Immediately highlights non-uniform or negative scaling directly in the Hierarchy before playtesting or baking.
  • Configurable Checks: Independently toggle non-uniform scale audits (CheckNonUniformScale) and negative scale audits (CheckNegativeScale).
  • Physics & Mesh Safety: Protects physics colliders and skinned meshes from subtle deformation artifacts.
  • Visual Alert Tint: Customize alert badge color (AlertColor) to fit studio visual standards.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

[HierarchyTransformWarning]
public class PhysicsObstacle : MonoBehaviour
{
    public float mass = 50f;
}

SpecificationValue

csharp
public HierarchyTransformWarningAttribute()
  • Parameterless constructor with both non-uniform scale and negative scale checks enabled by default.

5. Properties & Customization Options

PropertyTypeDefaultDescription
CheckNonUniformScalebooltrueWhen true, flags an alert if local scale components are non-uniform (X != Y != Z).
CheckNegativeScalebooltrueWhen true, flags an alert if any local scale component is negative (< 0).
AlertColorstring"#FF4757"Hex color code used for the transform warning alert badge.
XOffsetfloat4fHorizontal offset in pixels from the right margin of the hierarchy row.
IsActiveInPlayModebooltrueWhether transform warning auditing continues during active Play Mode.
Orderint0Drawing order priority relative to other hierarchy row renderers.

6. Real-World Production Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.HierarchyDesigner;

namespace ProjectZero.Physics
{
    // High-precision trigger volume requiring strictly uniform positive scaling
    [RequireComponent(typeof(SphereCollider))]
    [HierarchyTransformWarning(
        CheckNonUniformScale = true,
        CheckNegativeScale = true,
        AlertColor = "#E84118",
        XOffset = 6f)]
    public class ProximityRadarSphere : MonoBehaviour
    {
        public float scanRadius = 10f;
        public LayerMask detectionLayers;

        private void OnValidate()
        {
            // Optional editor clamp companion
            if (transform.localScale.x != transform.localScale.y || transform.localScale.y != transform.localScale.z)
            {
                Debug.LogWarning($"[ProximityRadarSphere] Non-uniform scale on {name} may distort sphere collision geometry!", this);
            }
        }
    }
}
